\omega\in C_{*}(\text{Conf}(D^{n});R)
such that
\partial\omega=\left[e,z\right]
.
Consider the map

H(x^{j}\otimes y)=(-1)^{\text{deg}(y)}\sum_{i=1}^{j}\binom{j}{i}x^{j-i}\otimes(m_{l})_{*}\circ EZ(\text{MConf}(f^{\prime})_{*}(\lambda^{i-1}(e,\omega))\otimes y).

We now check that
F=\partial H+H\partial
. We have that
\partial H(x^{j}\otimes y)
is equal to

(-1)^{\text{deg}(y)}\sum_{i=1}^{j}\binom{j}{i}x^{j-i}\otimes(m_{l})_{*}\circ EZ\big(\big((-1)^{\text{deg}(y)}\text{MConf}(f^{\prime})_{*}(\partial\lambda^{i-1}(e,\omega))\otimes y\big)+\text{MConf}(f^{\prime})_{*}(\lambda^{i-1}(e,\omega))\otimes\partial y\big).

We have that
\partial\lambda(e,\sigma)=\lambda(e,\partial\sigma)
for any chain
\sigma\in C_{*}(\text{Conf}(D^{n});R)
because
e
is a class of even degree. Therefore, we have that
\partial\lambda^{i-1}(e,\omega)=\lambda^{i-1}(e,\left[e,z\right])=\lambda^{i}(e,z)
and so

\partial H(x^{j}\otimes y)=F(x^{j}\otimes y)+(-1)^{\text{deg}(y)}\sum_{i=1}^{j}\binom{j}{i}x^{j-i}\otimes(m_{l})_{*}\circ EZ\big(\text{MConf}(f^{\prime})_{*}(\lambda^{i-1}(e,\omega))\otimes\partial y\big).

Since
H\partial(x^{j}\otimes y)=H(x^{j}\otimes\partial y)=(-1)^{\text{deg}(y)-1}\sum_{i=1}^{j}\binom{j}{i}x^{j-i}\otimes(m_{l})_{*}\circ EZ\big(\text{MConf}(f^{\prime})_{*}(\lambda^{i-1}(e,\omega))\otimes\partial y\big)
, we have that
F=\partial H+H\partial
.
∎

5.2.
Stabilization Maps for the Configuration Space of a Disk

In
Section

4.2
we constructed a semi-simplicial space
Y_{\bullet}
whose geometric realization is weakly equivalent to
\text{Mod-Conf}^{\,1}_{S^{n-1}}(\bar{D^{n}})
.
In this subsection, we use
Y_{\bullet}
to do something analogous on the chain level: we construct semi-simplicial chain complexes
U_{\bullet}
and
V_{\bullet}
whose totalizations
T(U)_{*}
and
T(V)_{*}
, respectively, are chain complexes quasi-isomorphic to
C_{*}(\text{Mod-Conf}^{\,1}_{S^{n-1}}(\bar{D^{n}});R)
. The chain complex
T(V)_{*}
has the structure of a right
C_{*}(\text{MConf}(S^{n-1});R)
-module. Then we construct stabilization maps on
T(U)_{*}
and
T(V)_{*}
. We use the stabilization map

t_{z}^{l}
on
C_{*}(\text{MConf}(S^{n-1});R)
from
Definition

5.5
to define a stabilization map
g_{z}^{l}
on
T(V)_{*}
that is also a map of right
C_{*}(\text{MConf}(S^{n-1});R)
-modules. Similarly, we use the stabilization maps
s_{z}^{l}
and
s_{z}^{r}
on
R[x]\otimes C_{*}(\text{Mod-Conf}^{\,1}_{S^{n-1}}(\bar{D}^{n});R)
from
Definition

5.6
to define stabilization maps
\gamma_{z}^{l}
and
\gamma_{z}^{r}
on
T(U)_{*}
. We conclude this subsection by showing that
\gamma_{z}^{l}
and
\gamma_{z}^{r}
are chain homotopic–we will need this in the proof of
Proposition

5.36
.

Definition 5.10
.

Let
V_{\bullet}^{\prime\prime}
denote the semi-simplicial chain complex whose
p
-simplices are

V_{p}^{\prime\prime}=\begin{cases}C_{*}(\text{Mod-Conf}^{\,1}_{S^{n-1}}(\bar{D}^{n})_{\leq 1};R)\otimes_{R}C_{*}(\text{MConf}(S^{n-1});R)&p=0\\
C_{*}(\text{MConf}(S^{n-1})_{1};R)\otimes_{R}C_{*}(\text{MConf}(S^{n-1});R)&p=1\\
\emptyset&p>1.\end{cases}

The face maps
d_{0},d_{1}\colon V_{1}^{\prime\prime}\to V_{0}^{\prime\prime}
are defined as follows: given
\sigma\otimes\tau\in V_{1}^{\prime\prime}
,
let

\displaystyle d_{0}(\sigma\otimes\tau)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}

\displaystyle(\emptyset,0)\otimes(-\cdot-)_{*}\circ EZ(\sigma\otimes\tau),\text{ and}

\displaystyle d_{1}(\sigma\otimes\tau)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}

\displaystyle\text{Conf}(\iota)_{*}(\sigma)\otimes\tau.

Let
V_{\bullet}^{\prime}\subset V_{\bullet}^{\prime\prime}
denote the sub-semi-simplicial chain complex with

V_{0}^{\prime}=V_{0}^{\prime\prime}
and

V_{1}^{\prime}=C_{*}((S^{n-1}\times\{1/2\})\times\{1\};R)\otimes_{R}C_{*}(\text{MConf}(S^{n-1});R).

Definition 5.11
.

Given a CW complex
X
,
let
C_{*}^{\text{cell}}(X;R)
denote the cellular chain complex of
X
with coefficients in
R
.

Definition 5.12
.

Given a semi-simplicial chain conplex
Z_{\bullet}
, with
Z_{p}=C_{p,*}
, let
(Z_{\bullet,*},\partial_{H},\partial_{V})
denote the double complex
Z_{p,q}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}C_{p,q}
, with the differential
\partial_{V}\colon Z_{\bullet,q}\to Z_{\bullet,q-1}
coming from the differential on the chain complex
Z_{p}
and the other differential
\partial_{H}\colon Z_{p,*}\to Z_{p-1,*}
coming from the alternating sum of the face maps
d_{i}\colon Z_{p}\to Z_{p-1}
.
By abuse of notation, let
\text{Tot}(Z_{\bullet})
denote the total complex of the double complex
(Z_{\bullet,*},\partial_{H},\partial_{V})
.

We will need the following results in
Lemma

5.17
.

Theorem 5.13

(see e.g.
[
FHT01
, Theorem 4.18]
)
.

Let
X
be a CW complex.
There is a quasi-isomorphism
C_{*}^{\text{cell}}(X;R)\to C_{*}(X;R)
.

Proposition 5.14

(see e.g.
[
BG91
, Proposition 1.2]
)
.

Let
X_{\bullet}
be a semi-simplicial space, and let
Z_{\bullet}
be the semi-simplicial chain complex with
Z_{p}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}C_{*}(X_{p};R)
and face maps on
Z_{\bullet}
induced from the face maps of
X_{\bullet}
. Then there is a quasi-isomorphism
\text{Tot}(Z_{\bullet})\to C_{*}(\|Z_{\bullet}\|;R)
.

Let
\mathbb{S}^{n-1}
denote
S^{n-1}
equipped with a CW-complex structure.

Definition 5.15
.

Fix a CW structure on
\mathbb{S}^{n-1}
using a single
0
-cell and a single
(n-1)
-cell only.
We map the
0
-cell to a point
p\in(S^{n-1}\times\{1/2\})\times\{1\}
and we map the
(n-1)
-cell to
(S^{n-1}\times\{1/2\})\times\{1\}
via the map induced by sending its boundary to
p
, so that we may identify
\mathbb{S}^{n-1}
with
(S^{n-1}\times\{1/2\})\times\{1\}\subset\text{MConf}(S^{n-1})_{1}
.
Let
V_{\bullet}\subset V_{\bullet}^{\prime}
denote the sub-semi-simplicial chain complex with

V_{0}=V_{0}^{\prime}
and

V_{1}=C^{\text{cell}}_{*}(\mathbb{S}^{n-1};R)\otimes_{R}C_{*}(\text{MConf}(S^{n-1});R)

Let
T(V)_{*}
denote
\text{Tot}(V_{\bullet})
.

Definition 5.16
.

Let
U_{\bullet}
denote the semi-simplicial chain complex with

U_{p}=\begin{cases}C_{*}(\text{Mod-Conf}^{\,1}_{S^{n-1}}(\bar{D}^{n})_{\leq 1};R)\otimes_{R}R[x]\otimes_{R}C_{*}(\text{Mod-Conf}^{\,1}_{S^{n-1}}(\bar{D}^{n});R)&p=0\\
C^{\text{cell}}_{*}(\mathbb{S}^{n-1};R)\otimes_{R}R[x]\otimes_{R}C_{*}(\text{Mod-Conf}^{\,1}_{S^{n-1}}(\bar{D}^{n});R)&p=1\\
0&p>1.\end{cases}

The face maps
d_{0},d_{1}\colon U_{1}\to U_{0}
are defined as follows: given
\sigma\otimes x^{j}\otimes\tau\in U_{1}
, let

d_{0}(\sigma\otimes x^{j}\otimes\tau)=\begin{cases}(\emptyset,0)\otimes x^{j+1}\otimes\tau&\text{deg}(\sigma)=n-1\\
(\emptyset,0)\otimes x^{j}\otimes\tau&\text{deg}(\sigma)=0\\
\end{cases}

and

d_{1}(\sigma\otimes x^{j}\otimes\tau)=\begin{cases}\text{Conf}(\iota)_{*}(\sigma)\otimes x^{j}\otimes\tau&\text{deg}(\sigma)=n-1\\
(\emptyset,0)\otimes x^{j}\otimes\tau&\text{deg}(\sigma)=0.\\
\end{cases}

Let
T(U)_{*}
denote
\text{Tot}(U_{\bullet})
.

Lemma 5.17
.

There is a quasi-isomorphism of right
C_{*}(\text{MConf}(S^{n-1});R)
-modules

T(V)_{*}\to C_{*}(\|Y_{\bullet}\|;R).

Proof.

We have that
V_{\bullet}^{\prime\prime}
has a right
C_{*}(\text{MConf}(S^{n-1});R)
-module structure coming from the right module structure of
C_{*}(\text{MConf}(S^{n-1});R)
.
The Eilenberg–Zilber map gives a levelwise quasi-isomorphism of semi-simplicial chain complexes
V_{\bullet}^{\prime\prime}\to C_{*}(Y_{\bullet};R)
. It is also a map of right
C_{*}(\text{MConf}(S^{n-1});R)
-modules since the Eilenberg–Zilber map is a lax monoidal functor and the right
C_{*}(\text{MConf}(S^{n-1});R)
-module structure on
V_{\bullet}^{\prime\prime}
and
Y_{\bullet}
comes from the monoid map on
\text{MConf}(S^{n-1})
. We also have levelwise quasi-isomorphisms of semi-simplicial chain complexes
V_{\bullet}^{\prime}\to V_{\bullet}^{\prime\prime}
and
V_{\bullet}\to V_{\bullet}^{\prime}
, since
S^{n-1}
is homotopy equivalent to
\text{MConf}(S^{n-1})_{1}
and
C^{\text{cell}}_{*}(\mathbb{S}^{n-1};R)
is quasi-isomorphic to
C_{*}(S^{n-1};R)
by
Theorem

5.13
. By taking the totalization of these semi-simplicial chain complexes, we have a quasi-isomorphism of right
C_{*}(\text{MConf}(S^{n-1});R)
-modules

T(V)_{*}\to\text{Tot}(C_{*}(Y_{\bullet};R))
. Finally the map
\text{Tot}(C_{*}(Y_{\bullet};R))\to C_{*}(\|Y_{\bullet}\|;R)
is a quasi-isomorphism by
Proposition

5.14
, and it is a map of right
C_{*}(\text{MConf}(S^{n-1});R)
-modules.
∎

Lemma 5.18
.

The map

(\text{id}\otimes\alpha)_{\bullet}\colon U_{\bullet}\to V_{\bullet},

where
\alpha\colon R[x]\otimes_{R}C_{*}(\text{Mod-Conf}^{\,1}_{S^{n-1}}(\bar{D}^{n});R)\to C_{*}(\text{MConf}(S^{n-1});R)
is the map from
Definition

5.3
, is a map of semi-simplicial chain complexes and it induces a quasi-isomorphism
((\text{id}\otimes\alpha)_{\bullet})_{*}\colon T(U)_{*}\to T(V)_{*}
.

Proof.

It is clear that
(\text{id}\otimes\alpha)_{\bullet}
is a map of semi-simplicial chain complexes. Since
\alpha
is a homotopy equivalence by
Lemma

5.4
, the map
(\text{id}\otimes\alpha)_{\bullet}
induces a quasi-isomorphism
((\text{id}\otimes\alpha)_{\bullet})_{*}
.
∎

Lemma 5.19
.

There is a composition of quasi-isomorphisms.

T(U)_{*}\to T(V)_{*}\to C_{*}(Y;R).

Proof.

By
Lemma

5.18
,
(\text{id}\otimes\alpha_{\bullet})_{*}\colon T(U)_{*}\to T(V)_{*}
is a quasi-isomorphism. Since there is a quasi-isomorphism
T(V)_{*}\to C_{*}(Y;R)
by
Lemma

5.17
, and the composition of quasi-isomorphisms is a quasi-isomorphism, we have that
T(U)_{*}
is quasi-isomorphic to
C_{*}(Y;R)
.
∎

Now we define stabilization maps

\displaystyle\gamma_{z}^{l},\gamma_{z}^{r}\colon T(U)_{*}

\displaystyle\to C_{*}(\text{Mod-Conf}^{\,1}_{S^{n-1}}(\bar{D}^{n});R)

\displaystyle g_{z}^{l}\colon T(V)_{*}

\displaystyle\to C_{*}(\text{Mod-Conf}^{\,1}_{S^{n-1}}(\bar{D}^{n});R).

To construct these stabilization maps, first observe that
U_{\bullet}
and
V_{\bullet}
only have simplices in degrees
0
and
1
. Therefore to construct
g^{l}_{z}
(likewise for
\gamma^{l}_{z}
and
\gamma^{r}_{z}
), it suffices to construct a map

g^{l}_{0,z}\colon C_{*}(V_{0};R)\to C_{*}(\text{Mod-Conf}^{\,1}_{S^{n-1}}(\bar{D}^{n});R)

and a homotopy
H
from
g^{l}_{0,z}\circ d_{0}
to
g^{l}_{0,z}\circ d_{1}
.

Definition 5.20
.

Let

g^{l}_{0,z}\colon V_{0}=C_{*}(\text{Mod-Conf}^{\,1}_{S^{n-1}}(\bar{D}^{n})_{\leq 1};R)\otimes C_{*}(\text{MConf}(S^{n-1});R)\to C_{*}(\text{Mod-Conf}^{\,1}_{S^{n-1}}(\bar{D}^{n});R)

be the composition
(m_{r})_{*}\circ EZ\circ\big((\text{id}_{\text{Mod-Conf}^{\,1}_{S^{n-1}}(\bar{D}^{n})})_{*}\otimes t^{l}_{z}\big)
.

Definition 5.21
.

Let

\gamma^{l}_{0,z},\gamma^{r}_{0,z}\colon C_{*}(\text{Mod-Conf}^{\,1}_{S^{n-1}}(\bar{D}^{n})_{\leq 1};R)\otimes R[x]\otimes C_{*}(\text{Mod-Conf}^{\,1}_{S^{n-1}}(\bar{D}^{n});R)\to C_{*}(\text{Mod-Conf}^{\,1}_{S^{n-1}}(\bar{D}^{n});R)

be the compositions
(m_{r})_{*}\circ EZ\circ\big(\text{id}\otimes(\alpha\circ s^{l}_{z})\big)
and
(m_{r})_{*}\circ EZ\circ\big(\text{id}\otimes(\alpha\circ s^{r}_{z})\big)
, respectively.

Since the maps
(m_{r})_{*}\circ EZ
and
t^{l}_{z}
are maps of right
C_{*}(\text{MConf}(S^{n-1});R)
-modules, the map
g^{l}_{0,z}
is a map of right
C_{*}(\text{MConf}(S^{n-1});R)
-modules.

Proposition 5.22
.

Let
R
be a ring
and let

e\in H_{0}(\text{Conf}_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n});R)
be the class of a point. Suppose that
z\in H_{*}(\text{Conf}(\mathbb{R}^{n});R)
is such that the Browder bracket
\left[e,z\right]=0
. Then the maps

g^{l}_{0,z}\circ d_{0},\text{ }g^{l}_{0,z}\circ d_{1}\colon V_{1}=C_{*}^{\text{cell}}(\mathbb{S}^{n-1};R)\otimes C_{*}(\text{MConf}(S^{n-1});R)\to C_{*}(\text{Mod-Conf}^{\,1}_{S^{n-1}}(D^{n});R)

are chain homotopic.

Proof.

Suppose
\sigma\otimes(\emptyset,0)\in V_{1}
.
It suffices to check that
g^{l}_{0,z}\circ d_{0}
and
g^{l}_{0,z}\circ d_{1}
are chain homotopic when restricting to
\sigma\otimes(\emptyset,0)\in V_{1}
, since
g^{l}_{0,z}\circ d_{0}
and
g^{l}_{0,z}\circ d_{1}
are maps of right
C_{*}(\text{MConf}(S^{n-1});R)
-modules.
We can represent
g^{l}_{0,z}\circ d_{0}(\sigma\otimes(\emptyset,0))
and
g^{l}_{0,z}\circ d_{1}(\sigma\otimes(\emptyset,0))
via
Figure

11
.

Figure 11.
The maps
g^{l}_{0,z}\circ d_{0}
and
g^{l}_{0,z}\circ d_{1}

We can express the image of
\sigma\otimes(\emptyset,0)
under these two maps as

\displaystyle g^{l}_{0,z}\circ d_{0}\colon

\displaystyle\sigma\otimes(\emptyset,0)\mapsto(\emptyset,0)\otimes(-\cdot-)_{*}\circ EZ(\sigma\otimes(\emptyset,0))\mapsto(\emptyset,0)\otimes t_{z}^{l}(\sigma)\mapsto(m_{r})_{*}\circ EZ\big((\emptyset,0)\otimes t_{z}^{l}(\sigma)\big),

\displaystyle g^{l}_{0,z}\circ d_{1}\colon

\displaystyle\sigma\otimes(\emptyset,0)\mapsto\text{Conf}(\iota)_{*}(\sigma)\otimes(\emptyset,0)\mapsto(m_{r})_{*}\circ EZ(\text{Conf}(\iota)_{*}(\sigma)\otimes t^{l}_{z}((\emptyset,0))).

Since
\sigma
is an element of
C_{*}^{\text{cell}}(\mathbb{S}^{n-1};R)
and
\mathbb{S}^{n-1}
only has one
0
-cell and one
(n-1)
-cell,
C_{*}^{\text{cell}}(\mathbb{S}^{n-1};R)
is generated by a cell in degree
0
and degree
n-1
.
As a result,
we have two cases to consider:

Case 1:

\sigma
is the class of a point in
H_{0}(S^{n-1};R)=C_{0}^{\text{cell}}(\mathbb{S}^{n-1};R)
, and

Case 2:

\sigma
is a generator of
H_{n-1}(S^{n-1};R)=C_{n-1}^{\text{cell}}(\mathbb{S}^{n-1};R)
.

Case 1:

Since
\sigma
is the class of a point,
we can construct a chain homotopy in this case by fixing a path
\omega_{0}
in
C_{*}(\text{Mod-Conf}^{\,1}_{S^{n-1}}(\bar{D}^{n});R)
that swaps
z
and the point in
Figure

11
.

Case 2:

If
\sigma
is a fundamental class in
H_{n-1}(S^{n-1};R)
, then
g^{l}_{0,z}\circ d_{1}(\sigma\otimes-)
is null-homotopic, since
S^{n-1}\hookrightarrow\bar{D}^{n}
being null-homotopic implies that
\text{Conf}(\iota)_{*}(\sigma)=\partial\omega^{\prime}
for some
\omega^{\prime}\in C_{*}(\text{Conf}(D^{n});R)
. Since

g_{0,z}^{l}\circ d_{0}(\sigma\otimes(\emptyset,0))=(m_{r})_{*}\circ EZ\big((\emptyset,0)\otimes t_{z}^{l}(\sigma)\big)=\left[e,z\right]

(see
Figure

5b
) and by assumption
\left[e,z\right]=0
, we have that
g^{l}_{0,z}\circ d_{1}(\sigma\otimes(\emptyset,0))=\partial\omega
for some chain
\omega
. Therefore, by picking a chain
\omega\in C_{*}(\text{Conf}(D^{n});R)
such that
\partial\omega=\left[e,z\right]
, we can construct a null homotopy for
g^{l}_{0,z}\circ d_{0}(\sigma\otimes(\emptyset,0))
. This gives a chain homotopy between
g^{l}_{0,z}\circ d_{0}
and

g^{l}_{0,z}\circ d_{1}
in this case as well. As a result, the map

\displaystyle H^{l}\colon C_{*}(V_{1};R)

\displaystyle\to C_{*}(\text{Mod-Conf}^{\,1}_{S^{n-1}}(\bar{D}^{n});R)

\displaystyle\sigma\otimes\tau

\displaystyle\mapsto\begin{cases}(m_{r})_{*}\circ EZ(\omega_{0}\otimes\tau)&\sigma\text{ a generator of }H_{0}(S^{n-1};R),\text{ and}\\
(m_{r})_{*}\circ EZ((\omega-\omega^{\prime})\otimes\tau)&\sigma\text{ a generator of }H_{n-1}(S^{n-1};R)\end{cases}

gives a chain homotopy from
g_{0,z}^{l}\circ d_{0}
to
g_{0,z}^{l}\circ d_{1}
.
∎

Definition 5.23
.

Let
e\in H_{0}(\text{Conf}_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n});R)
be the class of a point. Suppose that
z\in H_{*}(\text{Conf}(\mathbb{R}^{n});R)
is a homology class such that the Browder bracket
\left[z,e\right]=0
.
Let
g^{l}_{z}\colon T(V)_{*}\to C_{*}(\text{Mod-Conf}^{\,1}_{S^{n-1}}(\bar{D}^{n});R)
denote the
map on
T(V)_{*}
induced by taking the homotopy from
g^{l}_{0,z}\circ d_{0}
to
g^{l}_{0,z}\circ d_{1}
to be the homotopy
H^{l}
constructed at the end of
Proposition

5.22
.

Lemma 5.24
.

The maps
\gamma_{0,z}^{r}\circ d_{0}
and
\gamma_{0,z}^{r}\circ d_{1}
agree. If
\left[e,z\right]=0
, then the maps
\gamma_{0,z}^{l}\circ d_{0}
and
\gamma_{0,z}^{l}\circ d_{1}
are chain homotopic.

Proof.

Given
\sigma\otimes x^{j}\otimes y\in U_{1}
, without loss of generality, we can assume that the degree of
\sigma
is
n-1
, since
d_{0}
and
d_{1}
agree if the degree of
\sigma
is zero.
Since

(m_{r})_{*}\circ EZ(\text{Conf}(\iota)_{*}(\sigma)\otimes\alpha(x^{j}\otimes-))=(m_{r})_{*}\circ EZ((\emptyset,0)\otimes\alpha(x^{j+1}\otimes-)),

we have that
\gamma_{0,z}^{r}\circ d_{0}
and
\gamma_{0,z}^{r}\circ d_{1}
agree.

We now show that
\gamma_{0,z}^{l}\circ d_{0}
and
\gamma_{0,z}^{l}\circ d_{1}
are chain homotopic if
\left[e,z\right]=0
. We have that

\displaystyle\gamma_{0,z}^{l}d_{0}(\sigma\otimes x^{j}\otimes y)

\displaystyle=\gamma_{0,z}^{l}((\emptyset,0)\otimes x^{j+1}\otimes y)

\displaystyle=(m_{r})_{*}\circ EZ\big((\emptyset,0)\otimes(\alpha\circ s_{z}^{l})(x^{j+1}\otimes y)\big)

\displaystyle=(m_{r})_{*}\circ EZ\big((\emptyset,0)\otimes\alpha\big(\sum_{i=0}^{j+1}\binom{j+1}{i}x^{j+1-i}\otimes(m_{l})_{*}\circ EZ(\text{MConf}(f^{\prime})_{*}(\lambda^{i}(e,z))\otimes y)\big)\big)

and

\displaystyle\gamma_{0,z}^{l}d_{1}(\sigma\otimes x^{j}\otimes y)

\displaystyle=\gamma_{0,z}^{l}(\text{Conf}(\iota)_{*}(\sigma)\otimes x^{j}\otimes y)

\displaystyle=(m_{r})_{*}\circ EZ\big(\text{Conf}(\iota)_{*}(\sigma)\otimes(\alpha\circ s_{z}^{l})(x^{j}\otimes y)\big)

\displaystyle=(m_{r})_{*}\circ EZ\big(\text{Conf}(\iota)_{*}(\sigma)\otimes\alpha\big(\sum_{i=0}^{j}\binom{j}{i}x^{j-i}\otimes(m_{l})_{*}\circ EZ(\text{MConf}(f^{\prime})_{*}(\lambda^{i}(e,z))\otimes y)\big)\big).

Since
(m_{r})_{*}\circ EZ(\text{Conf}(\iota)_{*}(\sigma)\otimes\alpha(x^{j}\otimes-))=(m_{r})_{*}\circ EZ((\emptyset,0)\otimes\alpha(x^{j+1}\otimes-))
, we have that
\gamma_{0,z}^{l}d_{1}(\sigma\otimes x^{j}\otimes y)-\gamma_{0,z}^{l}d_{0}(\sigma\otimes x^{j}\otimes y)
is equal to

\displaystyle(m_{r})_{*}\circ EZ\big((\emptyset,0)\otimes\alpha\big(\sum_{i=0}^{j+1}\bigg(\binom{j+1}{i}-\binom{j}{i}\bigg)x^{j+1-i}\otimes(m_{l})_{*}\circ EZ(\text{MConf}(f^{\prime})_{*}(\lambda^{i}(e,z))\otimes y)\big)\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(m_{r})_{*}\circ EZ\big((\emptyset,0)\otimes\alpha\big(\sum_{i=1}^{j+1}\bigg(\binom{j+1}{i}-\binom{j}{i}\bigg)x^{j+1-i}\otimes(m_{l})_{*}\circ EZ(\text{MConf}(f^{\prime})_{*}(\lambda^{i}(e,z))\otimes y)\big)\big)

(by abuse of notation, when
i=j+1
we set
\binom{j}{j+1}
to be zero).
Since
\left[e,z\right]=0
, we can fix a chain
\omega\in C_{*}(\text{Mod-Conf}^{\,1}_{S^{n-1}}(\bar{D}^{n});R)
such that
\partial\omega=\left[e,z\right]
.
The map
H_{l}\colon U_{1}\to C_{*}(\text{Mod-Conf}^{\,1}_{S^{n-1}}(\bar{D}^{n});R)
, which sends
\sigma\otimes x^{j}\otimes y
to

(m_{r})_{*}\circ EZ\big((\emptyset,0)\otimes\alpha\big(\sum_{i=1}^{j+1}\bigg(\binom{j+1}{i}-\binom{j}{i}\bigg)x^{j+1-i}\otimes(m_{l})_{*}\circ EZ(\text{MConf}(f^{\prime})_{*}(\lambda^{i-1}(e,\omega))\otimes y)\big)\big)

if the degree of
\sigma
is
n-1
and which is zero otherwise,
gives a homotopy from
\gamma_{0,z}^{l}\circ d_{0}
to
\gamma_{0,z}^{l}\circ d_{1}
.
∎

Definition 5.25
.

Let
e\in H_{0}(\text{Conf}_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n});R)
be the class of a point. Suppose that
z\in H_{*}(\text{Conf}(\mathbb{R}^{n});R)
is a homology class such that the Browder bracket
\left[z,e\right]=0
.
Let

\gamma^{l}_{z}\colon T(U)_{*}\to C_{*}(\text{Mod-Conf}^{\,1}_{S^{n-1}}(\bar{D}^{n});R)

denote the
map on
T(U)_{*}
induced by taking the homotopy from
\gamma^{l}_{0,z}\circ d_{0}
to
\gamma^{l}_{0,z}\circ d_{1}
to be the homotopy
H_{l}
constructed at the end of
Lemma

5.24
.
Let
\gamma^{r}_{z}\colon T(U)_{*}\to C_{*}(\text{Mod-Conf}^{\,1}_{S^{n-1}}(\bar{D}^{n});R)
denote the map on
T(U)_{*}
induced by taking the homotopy from
\gamma^{r}_{0,z}\circ d_{0}
to
\gamma^{r}_{0,z}\circ d_{1}
to be the zero map.

Remark 5.26
.

The homotopies from
s_{z}^{l}
to
s_{z}^{r}
and from
\gamma_{0,z}^{l}\circ d_{0}
to
\gamma_{0,z}^{l}\circ d_{1}

both come from fixing a chain
\omega\in C_{*}(\text{Conf}(D^{n});R)
which satisfies
\partial\omega=\left[e,z\right]
. We will assume that the chain
\omega
is the same for both homotopies because we will need to assume this in the proof of
Lemma

5.27
.

Lemma 5.27
.

The maps
\gamma_{z}^{l}
and
\gamma_{z}^{r}
are chain homotopic.

Proof.

The data
of a map
f\colon T(U)_{*}\to D_{*}
, where
D_{*}
is a chain complex, is given by a pair
(f_{0},H_{f_{0}})
, where
f_{0}
is a map
f_{0}\colon U_{0}\to D_{*}
and
H_{f_{0}}
is a homotopy
H_{f_{0}}\colon U_{1}\to D_{*+1}
from
f_{0}\circ d_{0}
to
f_{0}\circ d_{1}
. Therefore, to give a chain homotopy between two maps
f=(f_{0},H_{f_{0}}),g=(g_{0},H_{g_{0}})\colon T(U)_{*}\to D_{*}
,
we need the following data:

(1)

a homotopy
H_{i}\colon U_{1}\to D_{*+1}
from
f_{0}\circ d_{i}
to
g_{0}\circ d_{i}
for each
i=0,1
, and

(2)

a map
K\colon U_{1}\to D_{*+2}
that satisfies
\partial K=H_{1}-H_{f}-H_{0}+H_{g}

By
Proposition

5.9
,
the map

H(x^{j}\otimes-)=\sum_{i=1}^{j}\binom{j}{i}x^{j-i}\otimes(m_{l})_{*}\circ EZ(\text{MConf}(f^{\prime})_{*}(\lambda^{i-1}(e,\omega))\otimes-)

gives a homotopy from
s_{z}^{l}
to
s_{z}^{r}
. Therefore,
the map

H^{l,r}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}(m_{r})_{*}\circ EZ\circ(\text{id}\otimes(\alpha\circ H))

gives a homotopy
from
\gamma_{0,z}^{l}
to
\gamma_{0,z}^{r}
. Composing
H^{l,r}
with
d_{i}
gives a homotopy
H_{i}

from
\gamma_{0,z}^{l}\circ d_{i}
to
\gamma_{0,z}^{r}\circ d_{i}
. We set
H_{l}
to be the homotopy
\gamma_{0,z}^{l}\circ d_{0}
to
\gamma_{0,z}^{l}\circ d_{1}
given at the end of
Lemma

5.24
. Since
\gamma_{0,z}^{l}\circ d_{0}=\gamma_{0,z}^{l}\circ d_{1}
by
Lemma

5.24
, we can set the homotopy
H_{r}
from
\gamma_{0,z}^{r}\circ d_{0}
to
\gamma_{0,z}^{r}\circ d_{1}
to be the zero map.
We now calculate
H_{1}-H_{0}+H_{l}
. Given
\sigma\otimes x^{j}\otimes y\in U_{1}
, if the degree of
\sigma
is zero then
(H_{1}-H_{0}+H_{l})(\sigma\otimes x^{j}\otimes y)=0
since
H_{l}(\sigma\otimes x^{j}\otimes y)=0
by definition and
d_{0}(\sigma\otimes x^{j}\otimes y)=d_{1}(\sigma\otimes x^{j}\otimes y)
. Suppose now that the degree of
\sigma
is
n-1
.

By definition, the maps
H_{l}
,
H_{0}
, and
H_{1}
send
\sigma\otimes x^{j}\otimes y
to

\displaystyle(m_{r})_{*}\circ EZ\big((\emptyset,0)\otimes\alpha(\sum_{i=1}^{j+1}\bigg(\binom{j+1}{i}-\binom{j}{i}\bigg)x^{j+1-i}\otimes(m_{l})_{*}\circ EZ(\text{MConf}(f^{\prime})_{*}(\lambda^{i-1}(e,\omega))\otimes y))\big),

\displaystyle(m_{r})_{*}\circ EZ\circ((\emptyset,0)\otimes\alpha(\sum_{i=1}^{j+1}\binom{j+1}{i}x^{j+1-i}\otimes(m_{l})_{*}\circ EZ(\text{MConf}(f^{\prime})_{*}(\lambda^{i-1}(e,\omega))\otimes y))),\text{ and}

\displaystyle(m_{r})_{*}\circ EZ\circ(\text{Conf}(\iota)_{*}(\sigma)\otimes\alpha(\sum_{i=1}^{j}\binom{j}{i}x^{j-i}\otimes(m_{l})_{*}\circ EZ(\text{MConf}(f^{\prime})_{*}(\lambda^{i-1}(e,\omega))\otimes y))),

respectively. Since
(m_{r})_{*}\circ EZ(\text{Conf}(\iota)_{*}(\sigma)\otimes\alpha(x^{j}\otimes-))=(m_{r})_{*}\circ EZ((\emptyset,0)\otimes\alpha(x^{j+1}\otimes-))
, we have that
(H_{1}-H_{0}+H_{l})(\sigma\otimes x^{j}\otimes y)=0
. It follows that we can set the map
K
to be the zero map.
∎

5.3.
Stabilization Maps for the Configuration Space of a Manifold

In this subsection, we give a model for
C_{*}(\text{Conf}(M);R)

by using copies of a model for
C_{*}(\text{Mod-Conf}^{\,1}_{S^{n-1}}(\bar{D}^{n});R)
, the chain complex
T(V)_{*}
from
Definition

5.15
. We will need to work with copies of
T(V)_{*}
instead of a single copy because this will allow us to define iterated mapping cones on our model for
C_{*}(\text{Conf}(M);R)
. Then we use the stabilization map
g^{l}_{z}
from
Definition

5.23
on
T(V)_{*}
to get a stabilization map
t_{z,c}
when
\left[z,e\right]=0
on our model for
C_{*}(\text{Conf}(M);R)
. We conclude by relating this new stabilization map to the stabilization map
t_{z}
when
M
is an open connected manifold.

Definition 5.28
.

Suppose that
U,V
, and
W
are spaces, with
V
a monoid,
U
a right
V
-module, and
W
a left
V
-module, and that
Z_{\bullet}
is the semi-simplicial space
B_{\bullet}(U,V,W)
. Let
N_{*}(Z_{\bullet};R)
denote
the semi-simplicial chain complex, whose
p
-simplices in degree
q
are

N_{q}(Z_{p};R)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\bigoplus_{q=q_{0}+\ldots+q_{p+1}}C_{q_{0}}(U;R)\otimes_{R}C_{q_{1}}(V;R)\otimes_{R}\cdots\otimes_{R}C_{q_{p}}(V;R)\otimes_{R}C_{q_{p+1}}(W;R).

Given
\sigma\in C_{q_{0}}(U;R)
,
\tau_{j}\in C_{q_{j}}(V;R)
, and
\omega\in C_{q_{p+1}}(W;R)
, the face maps
d_{i}\colon N_{q}(Z_{p};R)\to N_{q}(Z_{p-1};R)
for
0\leq i\leq p
send
\sigma\otimes\tau_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes\tau_{p}\otimes\omega
to

\displaystyle d_{0}(\sigma\otimes\tau_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes\tau_{p}\otimes\omega)

\displaystyle=((m_{r})_{*}\circ EZ(\sigma\otimes\tau_{1}))\otimes\tau_{2}\otimes\cdots\otimes\tau_{p}\otimes\omega

\displaystyle d_{i}(\sigma\otimes\tau_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes\tau_{p}\otimes\omega)

\displaystyle=\sigma\otimes\tau_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes\tau_{i-1}\otimes((-\cdot-)_{*}\circ EZ(\tau_{i}\otimes\tau_{i+1}))\otimes\tau_{i+2}\otimes\cdots\otimes\tau_{p}\otimes\omega,0<i<p,

\displaystyle d_{p}(\sigma\otimes\tau_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes\tau_{p}\otimes\omega)

\displaystyle=\sigma\otimes\tau_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes\tau_{p-1}\otimes(m_{l})_{*}\circ EZ(\tau_{p}\otimes\omega),

where
m_{r}\colon U\times V\to U
is the right module map of
U
,
(-\cdot-)
is the monoid map of
V
, and
m_{l}\colon V\times W\to W
is the left module map of
W
.
If
Z_{\bullet}^{\prime}\subset Z_{\bullet}
is a sub-semi-simplicial space, we will use
N_{*}(z^{\prime}_{\bullet};R)
to denote the analogous sub-semi-simplicial chain complex for
Z_{\bullet}^{\prime}
.

Lemma 5.29
.

Suppose
that
U,V
, and
W
are spaces, with
V
a monoid,
U
a right
V
-module, and
W
a left
V
-module.
There is a quasi-isomorphism

\text{Tot}(N_{*}(B_{\bullet}(U,V,W);R))\to C_{*}(\|B_{\bullet}(U,V,W)\|;R).

Proof.

By the Künneth theorem, the Eilenberg–Zilber map induces a quasi-isomorphism

\text{Tot}(N_{*}(B_{\bullet}(U,V,W);R))\to\text{Tot}(C_{*}(B_{\bullet}(U,V,W);R)).

We have a quasi-isomorphism

\text{Tot}(C_{*}(B_{\bullet}(U,V,W);R))\to C_{*}(\|B_{\bullet}(U,V,W)\|;R)

by
Proposition

5.14
.
Since the composition of quasi-isomorphisms is a quasi-isomorphism, there is a quasi-isomorphism
\text{Tot}(N_{*}(B_{\bullet}(U,V,W);R))\to C_{*}(\|B_{\bullet}(U,V,W)\|;R)
.
∎

Definition 5.30
.

Suppose that
M_{1}
and
M_{2}
are two manifolds with boundary and that
(N_{1},\ldots,N_{m})
is an
m
-tuple of disjoint subspaces contained in
\partial M_{1}
and
\partial M_{2}
. Let
N
denote
\bigsqcup_{j=1}^{m}N_{j}
. Let
C\mathcal{X}^{m}_{k,\bullet}({M_{1}},{N},{M_{2}};{R})
and
C\mathcal{X}^{m}_{\bullet}({M_{1}},{N},{M_{2}};{R})
denote the semi-simplicial chain complexes
N_{*}(\mathcal{X}_{k,\bullet}^{m}({M_{1}},{N},{M_{2}});R)
and

N_{*}(\mathcal{X}^{m}_{\bullet}({M_{1}},{N},{M_{2}});R)
, respectively.

Suppose that we have a map
A_{*}\to C_{*}(\text{Mod-Conf}^{\,m}_{N}(M_{1});R)
of right
\prod_{j=1}^{m}C_{*}(\text{MConf}(N_{j});R)
-modules.
By abuse of notation, let

C\mathcal{X}^{m}_{\bullet}({A_{*}},{N},{M_{2}};{R})
denote the semi-simplicial chain complex

C\mathcal{X}^{m}_{\bullet}({A_{*}},{N},{M_{2}};{R})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}N_{*}\big(B_{\bullet}(A_{*},\prod_{j=1}^{m}C_{*}(\text{MConf}(N_{j});R),C_{*}(\text{Mod-Conf}^{\,m}_{N}(M_{2});R))\big).

Let
C\mathcal{X}^{m}_{k,\bullet}({A_{*}},{N},{M_{2}};{R})\subset C\mathcal{X}^{m}_{\bullet}({A_{*}},{N},{M_{2}};{R})
denote the sub-semi-simplicial chain complex that maps to
C\mathcal{X}^{m}_{k,\bullet}({M_{1}},{N},{M_{2}};{R})
under the map
A_{*}\to C_{*}(\text{Mod-Conf}^{\,m}_{N}(M_{1});R)
.

Definition 5.31
.

Fix
m
disjoint closed disks
\bar{D}^{n}_{1},\ldots,\bar{D}^{n}_{m}
in
M
.
Let
\bar{K}_{m}
denote
\bigsqcup_{j=1}^{m}\bar{D}^{n}_{j}\subset M
and let
K_{m}
denote
\text{int}(\bar{K}_{m})
. Fix
m
copies
T(V^{1})_{*},\ldots,T(V^{m})_{*}
of
T(V)_{*}
.

Definition 5.32
.

Let
[n]
denote the set
\{1,\ldots,n\}
.
Given
I\subseteq[m]
, let
A^{I}_{*}
denote the chain complex

A^{I}_{*}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\bigoplus_{i\in I}T(V^{i})_{*}\bigoplus_{j\in I^{c}}C_{*}(\text{Mod-Conf}^{\,1}_{S^{n-1}_{j}}(\bar{D}^{n}_{j});R).

Definition 5.33
.

Let
e\in H_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{n};R)
be the class of a point.
Suppose that
(z_{1},\ldots,z_{d})
is a
d
-tuple of classes in
H_{*}(\text{Conf}(\mathbb{R}^{n});R)
such that
\left[z_{b},e\right]=0
for all
b=1,\ldots,d
.
Given
I\subseteq[m]
with
[d]\subseteq I
, for each
b=1,\ldots,d
, define

t_{z_{b},c}\colon\text{Tot}(C\mathcal{X}^{m}_{k,\bullet}({A^{I}_{*}},{\partial\bar{K}_{m}},{M\setminus K_{m}};{R}))\to\text{Tot}(C\mathcal{X}^{m}_{k+\text{par}(z_{b}),\bullet}({A^{I\setminus\{b\}}_{*}},{\partial\bar{K}_{m}},{M\setminus K_{m}};{R}))

to be the stabilization map induced from the map

g_{z_{b}}^{l}\colon T(V^{b})_{*}\to C_{*}(\text{Mod-Conf}^{\,1}_{S^{n-1}_{b}}(\bar{D}^{n}_{b});R)
from
Definition

5.23
, i.e. given

\displaystyle(\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{m})

\displaystyle\in\bigoplus_{i\in I}T(V^{i})_{*}\bigoplus_{j\in I^{c}}^{m}C_{*}(\text{Mod-Conf}^{\,1}_{S^{n-1}_{j}}(\bar{D}^{n}_{j});R)

\displaystyle\text{and }\tau

\displaystyle\in C_{*}(\prod_{q=1}^{m}\text{MConf}(S^{n-1}_{q});R)^{\otimes p}\otimes C_{*}(\text{Mod-Conf}^{\,m}_{\partial\bar{K}_{m}}(M\setminus K_{m});R),

t_{z_{b},c}((\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{m})\otimes\tau)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}(\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{b-1},g_{z_{b}}^{l}(\sigma_{b}),\sigma_{b+1},\ldots,\sigma_{m})\otimes\tau.

Remark 5.34
.

Since the map
g_{z}^{l}\colon T(V)_{*}\to C_{*}(\text{Mod-Conf}^{\,1}_{S^{n-1}}(\bar{D}^{n});R)
depends on fixing a chain homotopy from
g_{0,z}^{l}\circ d_{0}
to
g_{0,z}^{l}\circ d_{1}
, the map
t_{z,c}
also depends on this. If we fix a different chain homotopy, we obtain a different map
t_{z,c}^{\prime}
. We do not know if
t_{z,c}
and
t_{z,c}^{\prime}
are chain homotopic in general, but if
M
is an open connected manifold, we show that they are chain homotopic in
Proposition

5.36
.

Qualitatively, the stabilization map
t_{z_{i},c}
can be described as stabilizing in the
i
-th disk
D^{n}_{i}
.
We have that the stabilization maps
t_{z_{i},c}
from
Definition

5.33
commute with each other in the following sense: given
i\neq j
, the stabilization maps
g_{z_{i}}^{l}
and
g_{z_{j}}^{l}
also induce maps

\displaystyle t_{z_{i},c}^{\prime}\colon\text{Tot}(C\mathcal{X}^{m}_{k,\bullet}({A^{I\setminus\{j\}}_{*}},{\partial\bar{K}_{m}},{M\setminus K_{m}};{R}))

\displaystyle\to\text{Tot}(C\mathcal{X}^{m}_{k+\text{par}(z_{i}),\bullet}({A^{I\setminus\{i,j\}}_{*}},{\partial\bar{K}_{m}},{M\setminus K_{m}};{R}))\text{ and}

\displaystyle t_{z_{j},c}^{\prime}\colon\text{Tot}(C\mathcal{X}^{m}_{k,\bullet}({A^{I\setminus\{i\}}_{*}},{\partial\bar{K}_{m}},{M\setminus K_{m}};{R}))

\displaystyle\to\text{Tot}(C\mathcal{X}^{m}_{k+\text{par}(z_{j}),\bullet}({A^{I\setminus\{i,j\}}_{*}},{\partial\bar{K}_{m}},{M\setminus K_{m}};{R}))

respectively, and so we have that
t_{z_{i},c}^{\prime}t_{z_{j},c}=t_{z_{j},c}^{\prime}t_{z_{i},c}
.
Therefore, we can define an iterated mapping cone on
\text{Tot}(C\mathcal{X}^{m}_{k,\bullet}({A^{I}_{*}},{\partial\bar{K}_{m}},{M\setminus K_{m}};{R}))
as follows.

Definition 5.35
.

Suppose that
(z_{1},\ldots,z_{d})
is a
d
-tuple of classes in
H_{*}(\text{Conf}(\mathbb{R}^{n});R)
such that
\left[z_{b},e\right]=0
for all
b=1,\ldots,d
.
Given
I\subseteq[m]
with
[d]\subseteq I
,
recursively define the
d-th iterated mapping cone

\text{Cone}_{k}^{d,I}(M,(t_{z_{i},c}))
associated to the
d
-tuple
(t_{z_{1},c},\ldots,t_{z_{d},c})
and the subset
I
as follows: set
\text{Cone}_{k}^{1,I}(M,(t_{z_{1},c}))
to be the mapping cone

\text{Cone}\big(t_{z_{1},c}\colon\text{Tot}(C\mathcal{X}^{m}_{k-\text{par}(z_{1}),\bullet}({A^{I}_{*}},{\partial\bar{K}_{m}},{M\setminus K_{m}};{R}))\to\text{Tot}(C\mathcal{X}^{m}_{k,\bullet}({A^{I\setminus\{1\}}_{*}},{\partial\bar{K}_{m}},{M\setminus K_{m}};{R}))\big)

and for
d>1
, set
\text{Cone}_{k}^{d,I}(M,(t_{z_{i},c}))
to be the induced mapping cone

\text{Cone}\big(t_{z_{d},c}\colon\text{Cone}_{k-\text{par}(z_{d})}^{d-1,I}(M,(t_{z_{1},c},\ldots,t_{z_{d-1},c}))\to\text{Cone}_{k}^{d-1,I\setminus\{d\}}(M,(t_{z_{1},c},\ldots,t_{z_{d-1},c}))\big).

Let
\text{Cone}^{d,I}(M,(t_{z_{i},c}))
denote
\bigsqcup_{k=0}^{\infty}\text{Cone}_{k}^{d,I}(M,(t_{z_{i},c}))
.

Now we relate the stabilization map
t_{z}
to
t_{z,c}
when
M
is an open connected manifold. We have a composition of quasi-isomorphisms

\text{Tot}(C\mathcal{X}^{1}_{\bullet}({T(V)_{*}},{S^{n-1}},{M\setminus D^{n}};{R}))\to\text{Tot}(C\mathcal{X}^{1}_{\bullet}({\bar{D}^{n}},{S^{n-1}},{M\setminus D^{n}};{R}))\to C_{*}(\text{Conf}(M);R)

and a diagram

(5.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{Tot}(C\mathcal{X}^{1}_{\bullet}({T(V)_{*}},{S^{n-1}},{M\setminus D^{n}};{R}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{*}(\text{Conf}(M);R)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{Tot}(C\mathcal{X}^{1}_{\bullet}({\bar{D}^{n}},{S^{n-1}},{M\setminus D^{n}};{R}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{*}(\text{Conf}(M);R).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t_{z,c}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t_{z}}

We will show that this diagram commutes up to chain homotopy.
Roughly speaking, we will do this by using that certain stabilization maps on models of
C_{*}(\text{MConf}(S^{n-1});R)
and
C_{*}(\text{Mod-Conf}^{\,1}_{S^{n-1}}(\bar{D}^{n});R)
are chain homotopic (
Lemma

5.8
and
Lemma

5.27
, respectively). Then we carefully pick a disk in
M
and a model for
C_{*}(\text{Conf}(M);R)
so that we can actually relate
t_{z}
to
t_{z,c}
by using these stabilization maps on
C_{*}(\text{MConf}(S^{n-1});R)
and
C_{*}(\text{Mod-Conf}^{\,1}_{S^{n-1}}(\bar{D}^{n});R)
and the homotopies between them.

Proposition 5.36
.

Suppose that
M
is an open connected manifold.
Suppose that
z\in H_{*}(\text{Conf}(\mathbb{R}^{n});R)
is a class such that
\left[z,e\right]
is zero.
Diagram
5.1

commutes up to chain homotopy.

Proof.

First, we fix some notation. Without loss of generality, we can assume that
M
is the interior of a manifold
\bar{M}
with non-empty boundary (see, for example, page 41 of
[
MP15
]
). Fix an embedding
\iota\colon M\sqcup D^{n}\to M
as in
Lemma

3.1
(although we took the domain of
\iota
in
Lemma

3.1
to be
M\sqcup\mathbb{R}^{n}
, we can take the domain to be
M\sqcup D^{n}
by first fixing a homeomorphism
D^{n}\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
and composing). Fix a closed
n
-dimensional disk

\bar{D}\subset\bar{M}
whose interior
D
strictly contains
\iota(D^{n})
and such that
\partial\bar{M}\cap\partial\bar{D}=\bar{D}^{n-1}\mathrel{{=}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}}\bar{D}^{n-1}_{0}
. Let
D^{n-1}_{0}
denote the interior of
\bar{D}^{n-1}_{0}
and
let
M^{\prime}
denote
\bar{M}\setminus D^{n-1}_{0}
.
Let
\bar{D}^{n-1}_{1}
denote the boundary of
\bar{D}\setminus D^{n-1}_{0}
. Fix a closed disk
\bar{D}^{n}_{2}\subset D
not containing
D^{n}
.
We can summarize our setup with
Figure

12
.

Figure 12.
Regions of the manifold
\bar{M}

Replace
\bar{M}
by attaching a collar neighborhood
\partial\bar{M}\times[0,1]
to
\partial\bar{M}
along
\partial\bar{M}\times\{0\}
via the identity map and, by abuse of notation, denote this new space by
\bar{M}
. We attach a collar neighborhood
\partial\bar{M}\times\{0\}
so that we can consider the space
\text{Mod-Conf}^{\,1}_{S^{n-1}}(\bar{M}\cup_{\partial M}(\partial\bar{M}\times[0,1])\setminus D)
–otherwise the space
\bar{M}\setminus D
is not a manifold and so
\text{Mod-Conf}^{\,1}_{S^{n-1}}(\bar{M}\setminus D)
is not defined.
By

4.19
and the fact that
\text{Conf}(\bar{M})
and
\text{Conf}(M^{\prime})
are homotopy equivalent to
\text{Conf}(M)
, we have weak homotopy equivalences

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{X}^{1}_{\bullet}({\bar{D}^{n}},{S^{n-1}},{\bar{M}\setminus D})\|

\displaystyle\to\text{Conf}(M)

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{X}^{1}_{\bullet}({\bar{D}},{\bar{D}^{n-1}_{1}},{M^{\prime}\setminus D})\|

\displaystyle\to\text{Conf}(M).

We have that
\text{MConf}(\bar{D}^{n-1}_{1})
is a sub-monoid of
\text{MConf}(S^{n-1})
by identifying
\bar{D}^{n-1}_{1}
as a subspace of
\partial\bar{D}
.
As a result, there is an inclusion

\text{Mod-Conf}^{\,1}_{\bar{D}^{n-1}_{1}}(M^{\prime}\setminus D)\hookrightarrow\text{Mod-Conf}^{\,1}_{S^{n-1}}(\bar{M}\setminus D)

of
\text{MConf}(\bar{D}^{n-1}_{1})
-modules.
Therefore, we have the following commutative diagram, with the horizontal maps weak homotopy equivalences:
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\|\mathcal{X}^{1}_{\bullet}({\bar{D}},{\bar{D}^{n-1}_{1}},{M^{\prime}\setminus D})\|}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{Conf}(M)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\|\mathcal{X}^{1}_{\bullet}({\bar{D}^{n}},{S^{n-1}},{\bar{M}\setminus D})\|}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{Conf}(M).}

To prove that Diagram
5.1
commutes up to chain homotopy, it suffices to replace
M
with
\bar{M}
in Diagram
5.1
and show that the diagram

(5.2)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{Tot}(C\mathcal{X}^{1}_{\bullet}({T(V)_{*}},{S^{n-1}},{\bar{M}\setminus D};{R}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{*}(\text{Conf}(M);R)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{Tot}(C\mathcal{X}^{1}_{\bullet}({\bar{D}^{n}},{S^{n-1}},{\bar{M}\setminus D};{R}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{*}(\text{Conf}(M);R)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t_{z,c}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t_{z}}

commutes up to chain homotopy. In

, we now identify the
\bar{D}^{n}
in

with

, the

in

with

, and the

in

with

.

We now summarize the rest of the proof. Heuristically, we think of

as stabilizing by putting

inside

and

as stabilizing by bringing

into

from outside.
In

we also have the stabilization maps

and

from

, which we can use to compare

and

.
We use

and

and

on (a model for)

that are also maps of right

-modules. These new maps induce stabilization maps

and

on

. We use the homotopy from

to

in

to get an induced homotopy from

to

and then we relate

and

to

and
t_{z}
.

We introduce a sub-semi-simplicial chain complex of the semi-simplicial chain complex
V_{\bullet}
from
Definition

5.15
. Let
F
denote the chain complex

F\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\alpha(R[x]\otimes C_{*}(\text{Mod-Conf}^{\,1}_{S^{n-1}}(\bar{D}^{n});R))\otimes C_{*}(\text{MConf}(\bar{D}^{n-1}_{1});R).

From restricting the map

C_{*}(\text{MConf}(S^{n-1});R)\otimes C_{*}(\text{MConf}(S^{n-1});R)\to C_{*}(\text{MConf}(S^{n-1});R)

to

, we can view

as a right
C_{*}(\text{MConf}(\bar{D}^{n-1}_{1});R)
-submodule of
C_{*}(\text{MConf}(\partial\bar{D});R)
.
Note that
any map

extends to a map
\hat{g}\colon F\to F
of right
C_{*}(\text{MConf}(\bar{D}^{n-1}_{1});R)
-modules.
The construction of stabilization maps on

all depend on a fixed embedding

. We identify
D^{n-1}\times(0,1)
with a tubular neighborhood of

in

that contains
\iota(\mathbb{R}^{n})
and that does not intersect
\bar{D}^{n-1}_{1}
.
Let

denote the sub-semi-simplicial chain complex whose

-simplices are

\hat{U}_{p}=\begin{cases}C_{*}(\text{Mod-Conf}^{\,1}_{S^{n-1}}(\bar{D}^{n})_{\leq 1};R)\otimes F&p=0\\
C_{*}^{\text{cell}}(\mathbb{S}^{n-1};R)\otimes F&p=1.\\
\end{cases}

The inclusion
\text{Tot}(\hat{U}_{\bullet})\to T(V)_{*}
is a quasi-isomorphism
and a map of right
C_{*}(\text{MConf}(\bar{D}^{n-1}_{1});R)
-modules.

\text{Tot}(C\mathcal{X}^{1}_{\bullet}({\text{Tot}(\hat{U}_{\bullet})},{\bar{D}^{n-1}_{1}},{M^{\prime}\setminus D};{R}))\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\text{Tot}(C\mathcal{X}^{1}_{\bullet}({\text{Tot}(\hat{U}_{\bullet})},{\bar{D}^{n-1}_{1}},{M^{\prime}\setminus D};{R})).

We now construct stabilization maps on
\text{Tot}(C\mathcal{X}^{1}_{\bullet}({\text{Tot}(\hat{U}_{\bullet})},{\bar{D}^{n-1}_{1}},{M^{\prime}\setminus D};{R}))
.

Since the stabilization map

from

5.6
is a map from
R[x]\otimes C_{*}(\text{Mod-Conf}^{\,1}_{S^{n-1}}(\bar{D}^{n});R)
to itself,

induces a map
\hat{s}^{l}_{z}\colon F\to F
and the map
\gamma^{l}_{0,z}=(m_{r})_{*}\circ EZ\circ\big(\text{id}\otimes(\alpha\circ s^{l}_{z})\big)
induces a map of right
C_{*}(\text{MConf}(\bar{D}^{n-1}_{1});R)
-modules

\hat{\gamma}^{l}_{0,z}\colon\hat{U}_{0}\to C_{*}(\text{Mod-Conf}^{\,1}_{S^{n-1}}(\bar{D}^{n});R).

The homotopy
H_{l}
from

to

given in

5.24

induces a homotopy
\hat{H}_{l}
from
\hat{\gamma}^{l}_{0,z}\circ d_{0}
to
\hat{\gamma}^{l}_{0,z}\circ d_{1}
and which
is also a map of right
C_{*}(\text{MConf}(\bar{D}^{n-1}_{1});R)
-modules. Therefore, the map
\gamma^{l}_{z}\colon T(U)_{*}\to C_{*}(\text{Mod-Conf}^{\,1}_{S^{n-1}}(\bar{D}^{n});R)
induces a map of right
C_{*}(\text{MConf}(\bar{D}^{n-1}_{1});R)
-modules

\hat{\gamma}^{l}_{z}\colon\text{Tot}(\hat{U}_{\bullet})\to C_{*}(\text{Mod-Conf}^{\,1}_{S^{n-1}}(\bar{D}^{n});R).

Similarly, the map
\gamma^{r}_{z}\colon T(U)_{*}\to C_{*}(\text{Mod-Conf}^{\,1}_{S^{n-1}}(\bar{D}^{n});R)
induces a map of right
C_{*}(\text{MConf}(\bar{D}^{n-1}_{1});R)
-modules
\hat{\gamma}^{r}_{z}\colon\text{Tot}(\hat{U}_{\bullet})\to C_{*}(\text{Mod-Conf}^{\,1}_{S^{n-1}}(\bar{D}^{n});R)
.

Therefore, we have stabilization maps

\hat{t}^{l}_{z,c},\hat{t}^{r}_{z,c}\colon\text{Tot}(C\mathcal{X}^{1}_{\bullet}({\text{Tot}(\hat{U}_{\bullet})},{\bar{D}^{n-1}_{1}},{M^{\prime}\setminus D};{R}))\to\text{Tot}(C\mathcal{X}^{1}_{\bullet}({\bar{D}^{n}},{S^{n-1}},{\bar{M}\setminus D};{R}))

induced from
\hat{\gamma}^{l}_{z},\hat{\gamma}^{r}_{z}
, respectively. From the inclusions
\text{MConf}(\bar{D}^{n-1})\hookrightarrow\text{MConf}(S^{n-1})
and
\hat{U}_{\bullet}\hookrightarrow V_{\bullet}
, we have the following diagram.
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{Tot}(C\mathcal{X}^{1}_{\bullet}({\text{Tot}(\hat{U}_{\bullet})},{\bar{D}^{n-1}_{1}},{M^{\prime}\setminus D};{R}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{Tot}(C\mathcal{X}^{1}_{\bullet}({T(V)_{*}},{S^{n-1}},{\bar{M}\setminus D^{n}};{R}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{*}(\text{Conf}(M);R)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{Tot}(C\mathcal{X}^{1}_{\bullet}({\bar{D}^{n}},{S^{n-1}},{\bar{M}\setminus D};{R}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{*}(\text{Conf}(M);R)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{t}^{l}_{z,c},\hat{t}^{r}_{z,c}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t_{z,c}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t_{z}}

Since there is a quasi-isomorphism
\text{Tot}(C\mathcal{X}^{1}_{\bullet}({\text{Tot}(\hat{U}_{\bullet})},{\bar{D}^{n-1}_{1}},{M^{\prime}\setminus D};{R}))\to C_{*}(\text{Conf}(M^{\prime});R)
and the following composition

\text{Tot}(C\mathcal{X}^{1}_{\bullet}({\text{Tot}(\hat{U}_{\bullet})},{\bar{D}^{n-1}_{1}},{M^{\prime}\setminus D};{R}))\to\text{Tot}(C\mathcal{X}^{1}_{\bullet}({T(V)_{*}},{S^{n-1}},{\bar{M}\setminus D^{n}};{R}))\to C_{*}(\text{Conf}(M);R)

is a quasi-isomorphism,
the first map in this composition is a quasi-isomorphism
and it induces an isomorphism on sets of chain homotopy classes of maps

\displaystyle[\text{Tot}(C\mathcal{X}^{1}_{\bullet}({T(V)_{*}},{S^{n-1}},{\bar{M}\setminus D^{n}};{R})),\text{Tot}(C\mathcal{X}^{1}_{\bullet}({\bar{D}^{n}},{S^{n-1}},{\bar{M}\setminus D};{R}))]

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle[\text{Tot}(C\mathcal{X}^{1}_{\bullet}({\text{Tot}(\hat{U}_{\bullet})},{\bar{D}^{n-1}_{1}},{M^{\prime}\setminus D};{R})),\text{Tot}(C\mathcal{X}^{1}_{\bullet}({\bar{D}^{n}},{S^{n-1}},{\bar{M}\setminus D};{R}))]

.

Therefore, to prove that Diagram
5.2
commutes up to chain homotopy, it suffices to show that the diagrams

(5.3)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{Tot}(C\mathcal{X}^{1}_{\bullet}({\text{Tot}(\hat{U}_{\bullet})},{\bar{D}^{n-1}_{1}},{M^{\prime}\setminus D};{R}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{Tot}(C\mathcal{X}^{1}_{\bullet}({T(V)_{*}},{S^{n-1}},{\bar{M}\setminus D^{n}};{R}))]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{Tot}(C\mathcal{X}^{1}_{\bullet}({\bar{D}^{n}},{S^{n-1}},{\bar{M}\setminus D};{R}))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{t}^{l}_{z,c}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t_{z,c}}

and

(5.4)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{Tot}(C\mathcal{X}^{1}_{\bullet}({\text{Tot}(\hat{U}_{\bullet})},{\bar{D}^{n-1}_{1}},{M^{\prime}\setminus D};{R}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{*}(\text{Conf}(M);R)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{Tot}(C\mathcal{X}^{1}_{\bullet}({\bar{D}^{n}},{S^{n-1}},{\bar{M}\setminus D};{R}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{*}(\text{Conf}(M);R)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{t}^{r}_{z,c}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t_{z}}

commute up to chain homotopy and that
\hat{t}^{l}_{z,c}
and
\hat{t}^{r}_{z,c}
are chain homotopic.

Recall that
t_{z,c}
is induced from the map

g_{0,z}^{l}=(m_{r})_{*}\circ EZ\circ\big((\text{id}_{\text{Mod-Conf}^{\,1}_{S^{n-1}}(\bar{D}^{n})})_{*}\otimes t^{l}_{z}\big)\colon V_{0}\to C_{*}(\text{Mod-Conf}^{\,1}_{S^{n-1}}(\bar{D}^{n});R).

Since
\alpha\circ s^{l}_{z}
is homotopic to
t^{l}_{z}\circ\alpha
by
Lemma

5.8
, the restriction of

t_{z}^{l}\colon C_{*}(\text{MConf}(S^{n-1});R)\to C_{*}(\text{MConf}(S^{n-1});R)

to
F
is homotopic to
\hat{s}^{l}_{z}
. As a result,
Diagram
5.3
commutes up to chain homotopy.
Since the space of orientation-preserving embeddings from
D^{n}
to itself is path-connected, the embedding
f^{\prime}\colon D^{n}\to D^{n-1}_{0}\times(0,1)
is homotopic to
\iota|_{D^{n}}\colon D^{n}\to D^{n-1}_{0}\times(0,1)
. As a result,
Diagram
5.4

commutes up to chain homotopy.
The homotopy from
\gamma^{l}_{z}
to
\gamma^{r}_{z}
given in
Lemma

5.27
induces a homotopy from
\hat{\gamma}^{l}_{z}
to
\hat{\gamma}^{r}_{z}
that is also a right
C_{*}(\text{MConf}(\bar{D}^{n-1}_{1});R)
-module map. Therefore,
\hat{t}^{l}_{z,c}
and
\hat{t}^{r}_{z,c}
are chain homotopic.
∎

Corollary 5.37
.

Suppose that
z\in H_{*}(\text{Conf}(\mathbb{R}^{n});R)
is a class such that
\left[z,e\right]
is zero. There is a natural isomorphism

H_{*}\big(\text{Cone}(g^{l}_{z}\colon T(V)_{*}\to C_{*}(\text{Mod-Conf}^{\,1}_{S^{n-1}}(\bar{D}^{n});R))\big)\to H_{*}(\text{Cone}^{1}(D^{n},(t_{z}));R).

Corollary 5.38
.

Suppose that
M
is an open connected manifold
and that
z\in H_{*}(\text{Conf}(\mathbb{R}^{n});R)
is a class such that
\left[z,e\right]
is zero. If
t_{z}
induces an isomorphism on
H_{*}(\text{Conf}(M);R)
in a range, then
t_{z,c}
induces an isomorphism on
H_{*}(\text{Conf}(M);R)
in the same range.

Remark 5.39
.

Suppose that
M
is an open connected manifold of dimension
n
and that
z\in H_{i}(\text{Conf}(\mathbb{R}^{n});R)
is a class such that
\left[z,e\right]=0
.
By
Proposition

5.36
,
t_{z}
and
t_{z,c}
induce the same map on homology. Since the map

t_{z,c}\colon H_{*}(\text{Tot}(C\mathcal{X}^{1}_{\bullet}({T(V)_{*}},{S^{n-1}},{M\setminus D^{n}};{R})))\to H_{*}(\text{Tot}(C\mathcal{X}^{1}_{\bullet}({\bar{D}^{n}},{S^{n-1}},{M\setminus D^{n}};{R})))

does not depend on an embedding
\iota\colon M\sqcup\mathbb{R}^{n}\to M
, the stabilization map

t_{z}\colon H_{*}(\text{Conf}(M);R)\to H_{*}(\text{Conf}(M);R)

does not depend on a choice of an embedding
M\sqcup\mathbb{R}^{n}\to M
.

6.
The Puncture Resolution

We prove stability using a puncture resolution argument inspired by
[
Ran13a
, Section 9.4]
. Our puncture resolution is slightly more similar to the puncture resolution of
[
KM16
, Section 3]
than to that of
[
Ran13a
, Section 9.4]
. The puncture resolution is a semi-simplicial resolution
(Z^{m}_{k,\bullet}(M),\text{Conf}_{k}(M),\epsilon)
of
\text{Conf}_{k}(M)

whose simplices
involve configuration spaces of open manifolds. We then pass to chain complexes to construct an analogous semi-simplicial chain complex
CZ^{m}_{k,\bullet}(A^{I}_{*},M;R)
whose simplices involve chain complexes of open manifolds and whose totalization
\text{Tot}(CZ^{m}_{k,\bullet}(A^{I}_{*},M;R))
is quasi-isomorphic to
C_{*}(\text{Conf}_{k}(M);R)
. By the geometric realization spectral sequence, we can compare the homology of the
p
-simplices
CZ^{m}_{k,p}(A^{I}_{*},M;R)
to the homology of
C_{*}(\text{Conf}_{k}(M);R)
and leverage stability results for configuration spaces of open manifolds to prove analogous results for configuration spaces of closed manifolds.

Definition 6.1
.

Given a space
X
, let

\text{OConf}^{\,\delta}_{k}(X)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{k})\subset X^{k}\colon x_{i}\neq x_{j}\text{ if }i\neq j\}

denote the
set
of ordered configurations of
X
of cardinality
k
. We do
not
use the topology on
\text{OConf}^{\,\delta}_{k}(X)
as a subspace of
X^{k}
.

Definition 6.2
.

Let
\bar{K}_{m}
and
K_{m}
be as in
Definition

5.31
.
Let
Z^{m}_{k,\bullet}(M)
be the semi-simplicial space whose
p
-simplices are

Z^{m}_{k,p}(M)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\bigsqcup_{(m_{0},\ldots m_{p})\in\text{OConf}^{\,\delta}_{p+1}(M\setminus\bar{K}_{m})}\|\mathcal{X}_{k,\bullet}^{m}({\bar{K}_{m}},{\partial\bar{K}_{m}},{M\setminus K_{m}\setminus\{m_{0},\ldots m_{p}\}})\|.

The inclusions
M\setminus\{m_{0},\ldots,m_{p}\}\hookrightarrow M\setminus\{m_{0},\ldots,\widehat{m_{j}},\ldots,m_{p}\},
for
j=0,\ldots,p
, induce the face maps for
Z^{m}_{k,\bullet}(M)
. Let
Z^{m}_{\bullet}(M)
denote the semi-simplicial space

Z^{m}_{\bullet}(M)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\bigsqcup_{k=0}^{\infty}Z^{m}_{k,\bullet}(M).

The map
\epsilon\colon Z^{m}_{0}(M)\to\|\mathcal{X}^{m}_{\bullet}({\bar{K}_{m}},{\partial\bar{K}_{m}},{M\setminus K_{m}})\|
which forgets the ordered configuration
(m_{0})
satisfies
\epsilon d_{0}=\epsilon d_{1}
, so
(Z^{m}_{\bullet}(M),\|\mathcal{X}^{m}_{\bullet}({\bar{K}_{m}},{\partial\bar{K}_{m}},{M\setminus K_{m}})\|,\epsilon)
is an augmented semi-simplicial space. To prove that it is a semi-simplicial resolution of
\|\mathcal{X}^{m}_{\bullet}({\bar{K}_{m}},{\partial\bar{K}_{m}},{M\setminus K_{m}})\|
, we use a microfibration technique and a result from
[
GR18
]
.

Definition 6.3
.

A map
g\colon E\to B
of spaces is a
Serre microfibration
if for any
k
and any commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bar{D}^{k}\times\{0\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bar{D}^{k}\times\left[0,1\right]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H}

there exists an
\epsilon>0
and a map
\tilde{H}\colon\bar{D}^{k}\times\left[0,\epsilon\right]\to E
with
\tilde{H}(x,0)=h(x)
and
g\circ\tilde{H}(x,t)=H(x,t)
for all
(x,t)\in\bar{D}^{k}\times\left[0,\epsilon\right]
.

Lemma 6.4

(
[
Wei05
, Lemma 2.2]
)
.

Suppose that
g\colon E\to B
is a Serre microfibration. If
g
has weakly contractible fibers, then it is a weak homotopy equivalence.

Proposition 6.5

(
[
GR18
, Proposition 2.8]
)
.

Let
W_{\bullet}
be a semi-simplicial set, and
Z
be a Hausdorff space. Let
X_{\bullet}\subset Z\times W_{\bullet}
be a sub-semi-simplicial space which in each degree is an open subset. Then the map
\pi\colon\|X_{\bullet}\|\to Z
is a Serre microfibration.

Lemma 6.6
.

The map

\|\epsilon\|\colon\|Z^{m}_{\bullet}(M)\|\to\|\mathcal{X}^{m}_{\bullet}({\bar{K}_{m}},{\partial\bar{K}_{m}},{M\setminus K_{m}})\|

is a Serre microfibration.

Proof.

Let
W_{\bullet}
denote the semi-simplicial set whose
p
-simplices are
\text{OConf}^{\,\delta}_{p+1}(M\setminus\bar{K}_{m})
and whose face maps
d_{j}\colon W_{p}\to W_{p-1}
send
(m_{0},\ldots,m_{p})\in W_{p}
to
(m_{0},\ldots,\widehat{m_{j}},\ldots,m_{p})
. We can represent an element of
Z^{m}_{p}(M)
by a pair

\displaystyle((m_{0},\ldots,m_{p}),\mathcal{C}),

\displaystyle\text{ with }(m_{0},\ldots,m_{p})\in\text{OConf}^{\,\delta}_{p+1}(M\setminus\bar{K}_{m})

\displaystyle\text{ and }\mathcal{C}\in\|\mathcal{X}^{m}_{\bullet}({\bar{K}_{m}},{\partial\bar{K}_{m}},{M\setminus K_{m}\setminus\{m_{0},\ldots m_{p}\}})\|.

We have a natural open inclusion

\text{Mod-Conf}^{\,m}_{\partial\bar{K}_{m}}(M\setminus K_{m}\setminus\{m_{0},\ldots m_{p}\})\hookrightarrow\text{Mod-Conf}^{\,m}_{\partial\bar{K}_{m}}(M\setminus K_{m})

that is also a map of left
\prod_{j=1}^{m}\text{MConf}(S^{n-1}_{j})
-modules. As a result, we have an induced open inclusion

f\colon\|\mathcal{X}^{m}_{\bullet}({\bar{K}_{m}},{\partial\bar{K}_{m}},{M\setminus K_{m}\setminus\{m_{0},\ldots,m_{p}\}})\|\hookrightarrow\|\mathcal{X}^{m}_{\bullet}({\bar{K}_{m}},{\partial\bar{K}_{m}},{M\setminus K_{m}})\|.

Therefore, the map

\displaystyle g_{p}\colon Z^{m}_{p}(M)

\displaystyle\to W_{p}\times\|\mathcal{X}^{m}_{\bullet}({\bar{K}_{m}},{\partial\bar{K}_{m}},{M\setminus K_{m}})\|

\displaystyle((m_{0},\ldots,m_{p}),\mathcal{C})

\displaystyle\mapsto((m_{0},\ldots,m_{p}),f(\mathcal{C})).

is an open inclusion for all
p
.
The maps
g_{p}
assemble to define a map of semi-simplicial spaces

g_{\bullet}\colon Z^{m}_{\bullet}(M)\to W_{\bullet}\times\|\mathcal{X}^{m}_{\bullet}({\bar{K}_{m}},{\partial\bar{K}_{m}},{M\setminus K_{m}})\|.

Therefore, by
Proposition

6.5
the map

\|\epsilon\|\colon\|Z^{m}_{\bullet}(M)\|\to\|\mathcal{X}^{m}_{\bullet}({\bar{K}_{m}},{\partial\bar{K}_{m}},{M\setminus K_{m}})\|

is a Serre microfibration.
∎

Lemma 6.7
.

There is a weak homotopy equivalence

\rho\colon\|Z^{m}_{\bullet}(M)\|\to\text{Conf}(M)
.

Proof.

Our argument is similar to the proof of
[
KMT16
, Proposition 5.8]
, so we just summarize it. The map
\|\epsilon\|\colon\|Z^{m}_{\bullet}(M)\|\to\|\mathcal{X}^{m}_{\bullet}({\bar{K}_{m}},{\partial\bar{K}_{m}},{M\setminus K_{m}})\|
is a Serre microfibration by
Lemma

6.6
. Any fiber of this map is contractible because it is isomorphic to the complex of injective words for an infinite set
M\setminus\bar{K}_{m}\setminus\{m_{1},\ldots m_{k}\}
, with
m_{1},\ldots,m_{k}
a finite set of points in
M\setminus\bar{K}_{m}
. Therefore, by

Lemma

6.4
, the map
\|\epsilon\|
is a weak homotopy equivalence.
By
Corollary

4.19
, we have a weak homotopy equivalence
g\colon\|\mathcal{X}^{m}_{\bullet}({\bar{K}_{m}},{\partial\bar{K}_{m}},{M\setminus K_{m}})\|\to\text{Conf}(M)
. Since the composition of weak homotopy equivalences is a weak homotopy equivalence, the map
\rho\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}g\circ\|\epsilon\|
is a weak homotopy equivalence.
∎

Definition 6.8
.

Given
I\subseteq[m]
, let
CZ^{m}_{k,\bullet}(A^{I}_{*},M;R)
be the semi-simplicial chain complex
whose
p
-simplices are

CZ^{m}_{k,p}(A^{I}_{*},M;R)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\bigsqcup_{(m_{0},\ldots m_{p})\in\text{OConf}^{\,\delta}_{p+1}(M\setminus\bar{K}_{m})}\text{Tot}(C\mathcal{X}^{m}_{k,\bullet}({A^{I}_{*}},{\partial\bar{K}_{m}},{M\setminus K_{m}\setminus\{m_{0},\ldots m_{p}\}};{R}))

and let
CZ^{m}_{\bullet}(A^{I}_{*},M;R)
denote the semi-simplicial chain complex
\bigsqcup_{k=0}^{\infty}CZ^{m}_{k,\bullet}(A^{I}_{*},M;R)
.
The inclusions
M\setminus\{m_{0},\ldots,m_{p}\}\hookrightarrow M\setminus\{m_{0},\ldots,\widehat{m_{j}},\ldots,m_{p}\},
for
j=0,\ldots,p
, induce the face maps for both semi-simplicial chain complexes.

Similar to the augmentation map for
Z^{m}_{\bullet}(M)
, we have a map

\epsilon^{\prime}\colon CZ^{m}_{0}(A^{I}_{*},M;R)\to\text{Tot}(C\mathcal{X}^{m}_{\bullet}({A^{I}_{*}},{\partial\bar{K}_{m}},{M\setminus K_{m}};{R}))

induced from the map which forgets the ordered configuration
m_{0}\in\text{OConf}^{\,\delta}_{1}(M\setminus\bar{K}_{m})
. As a consequence of
\epsilon^{\prime}
and the naturality of the Eilenberg–Zilber map, we have a composition of quasi-isomorphisms

\text{Tot}(CZ^{m}_{\bullet}(A^{I}_{*},M;R))\to\text{Tot}(C\mathcal{X}^{m}_{\bullet}({A^{I}_{*}},{\partial\bar{K}_{m}},{M\setminus K_{m}};{R}))\to C_{*}(\text{Conf}(M);R).

Lemma 6.9
.

Suppose that
(z_{1},\ldots,z_{d})
is a
d
-tuple of classes in
H_{*}(\text{Conf}(\mathbb{R}^{n});R)
such that
\left[z_{b},e\right]=0
for all
b=1,\ldots,d
.
Given
I\subseteq[m]
with
[d]\subseteq I
, the stabilization maps

t_{z_{b},c}\colon\text{Tot}(C\mathcal{X}^{m}_{k-\text{par}(z_{b}),\bullet}({A^{I}_{*}},{\partial\bar{K}_{m}},{M\setminus K_{m}};{R}))\to\text{Tot}(C\mathcal{X}^{m}_{k,\bullet}({A^{I\setminus\{b\}}_{*}},{\partial\bar{K}_{m}},{M\setminus K_{m}};{R}))

from
Definition

5.33
extend to maps

(t_{z_{b},c})_{\bullet}\colon CZ^{m}_{k-\text{par}(z_{b}),\bullet}(A^{I}_{*},M;R)\to CZ^{m}_{k,\bullet}(A^{I\setminus\{b\}}_{*},M;R)

that all commute with each other.

Proof.

Let
\epsilon^{\prime}_{p}\colon CZ^{m}_{p}(A^{I}_{*},M;R)\to C\mathcal{X}^{m}_{p}({A^{I}_{*}},{\partial\bar{K}_{m}},{M\setminus K_{m}};{R})
denote the chain map coming from the map
\epsilon^{\prime}
. We can represent a chain in
CZ^{m}_{p}(A^{I}_{*},M;R)
by a pair

((m_{0},\ldots,m_{p}),\sigma)
with
(m_{0},\ldots,m_{p})\in\text{OConf}^{\,\delta}_{p+1}(M\setminus K_{m})
and
\sigma\in C\mathcal{X}^{m}_{\bullet}({A^{I}_{*}},{\partial\bar{K}_{m}},{M\setminus K_{m}\setminus\{m_{0},\ldots m_{p}\}};{R})
.
Since the points
m_{0},\ldots,m_{p}
are not in
\bar{K}_{m}
, we have that

t_{z_{b},c}\circ\epsilon^{\prime}_{p}(\sigma)\in C\mathcal{X}^{m}_{\bullet}({A^{I\setminus\{b\}}_{*}},{\partial\bar{K}_{m}},{M\setminus K_{m}\setminus\{m_{0},\ldots,m_{p}\}};{R}).

Therefore,

(t_{z_{b},c})_{p}((m_{0},\ldots,m_{p}),\sigma)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}(t_{z_{b},c}\circ\epsilon_{p}^{\prime}(\sigma),(m_{0},\ldots,m_{p}))

is a chain in
CZ^{m}_{p}(A^{I\setminus\{b\}}_{*},M;R)
.

To show that the maps
(t_{z_{b},c})_{p}
assemble to give a map

(t_{z_{b},c})_{\bullet}\colon CZ^{m}_{\bullet}(A^{I}_{*},M;R)\to CZ^{m}_{\bullet}(A^{I\setminus\{b\}}_{*},M;R)

of semi-simplicial chain complexes, we need to show that the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces CZ^{m}_{p}(A^{I}_{*},M;R)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces CZ^{m}_{p-1}(A^{I}_{*},M;R)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces CZ^{m}_{p}(A^{I\setminus\{b\}}_{*},M;R)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces CZ^{m}_{p-1}(A^{I\setminus\{b\}}_{*},M;R)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(t_{z_{b},c})_{p}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{q}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(t_{z_{b},c})_{p-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{q}}

commutes. Since the restriction of
d_{q}
to
C\mathcal{X}^{m}_{\bullet}({A^{I}_{*}},{\partial\bar{K}_{m}},{M\setminus K_{m}\setminus\{m_{0},\ldots,m_{p}\}};{R})
commutes with
(t_{z_{b},c})_{\bullet}
for all
m_{0},\ldots,m_{p}\in M\setminus\bar{K}_{m}
, i.e.

d_{q}\circ(t_{z_{b},c})_{p}=(t_{z_{b},c})_{p-1}\circ d_{q}
, it follows that the diagram commutes as well. The maps
(t_{z_{b},c})_{\bullet}
all commute with each other due to how
(t_{z_{b},c})_{\bullet}
was constructed and the stabilization maps
t_{z_{b},c}
all commuting with each other.
∎

Definition 6.10
.

Suppose that
(z_{1},\ldots,z_{d})
is a
d
-tuple of classes in
H_{*}(\text{Conf}(\mathbb{R}^{n});R)
such that
\left[z_{b},e\right]=0
for all
b=1,\ldots,d
.
Given
I\subseteq[m]
with
[d]\subseteq I
, let
\hat{t}_{z_{b},c}
denote the induced map
\text{Tot}((t_{z_{b},c})_{\bullet})
obtained by passing to the totalization of
CZ^{m}_{\bullet}(A^{I}_{*},M;R)
.
Recursively define the
d-th iterated mapping cone

\text{Cone}_{k}^{d,I}(M,(\hat{t}_{z_{i},c}))
associated to the
d
-tuple
(z_{1},\ldots,z_{d})
and the subset
I
as follows:
set
\text{Cone}_{k}^{1,I}(M,(\hat{t}_{z_{1},c}))
to be the mapping cone

\text{Cone}\big(\hat{t}_{z_{1},c}\colon\text{Tot}(CZ^{m}_{k-\text{par}(z_{1}),\bullet}(A^{I}_{*},M;R))\to\text{Tot}(CZ^{m}_{k,\bullet}(A^{I\setminus\{1\}}_{*},M;R))\big)

and for
d>1
, set
\text{Cone}_{k}^{d,I}(M,(\hat{t}_{z_{i},c}))
to be the induced mapping cone

\text{Cone}\big(\hat{t}_{z_{d},c}\colon\text{Cone}_{k-\text{par}(z_{d})}^{d-1,I}(M,(\hat{t}_{z_{1},c},\ldots,\hat{t}_{z_{d-1},c}))\to\text{Cone}_{k}^{d-1,I\setminus\{d\}}(M,(\hat{t}_{z_{1},c},\ldots,\hat{t}_{z_{d-1},c}))\big).

The following lemma lists some homology classes
z\in H_{*}(\text{Conf}(\mathbb{R}^{n});R)
for which
\left[z,e\right]=0
.

Lemma 6.11
.

Let
e\in H_{0}(\text{Conf}_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n});R)
be the class of a point.

(1)

If
R
is
\mathbb{F}_{p}
and
\omega\in H_{*}(\text{Conf}(\mathbb{R}^{n});\mathbb{F}_{p})
is a homology class such that
\omega=z^{p}
for some class
z
, then
\left[\omega,e\right]=0
. It follows that
\left[e^{p},e\right]=0
for all
n
.

(2)

If
R
is
\mathbb{F}_{p}
and
\omega\in H_{*}(\text{Conf}(\mathbb{R}^{n});\mathbb{F}_{p})
, then
\left[Q^{s}\omega,e\right]=0
.

(3)

If
R
is
\mathbb{F}_{2}
or
n
is odd, then
\left[e,e\right]=0
.

(4)

If
R
is
\mathbb{F}_{p}
and
x_{i},y_{i},z_{i}\in H_{*}(\text{Conf}(\mathbb{R}^{2});\mathbb{F}_{p})
are the homology classes from
Theorem

2.8
, then
\left[x_{i},e\right]=\left[y_{i},e\right]=\left[z_{i},e\right]=0
.

Proof.

Parts
1
and
3
appear in
[
KM16
, Proposition 2.5]
and
[
Ran13a
, p. 16]
, respectively. Parts
1
and
2
are immediate consequences of the derivation property of the Browder bracket (Property
4
) and the relationship of a Dyer-Lashof operation with a Browder bracket (Property
6
), respectively.
Part
3
follows
from the geometric description of the Browder bracket. The class
\left[e,e\right]
corresponds to twice a generator of
H_{n-1}(\mathbb{R}P^{n-1};R)
, so it vanishes when
n
is odd or
R=\mathbb{F}_{2}
.

For Part
4
, we first show that
\left[x_{i},e\right]
vanishes. Recall that
x_{i}
is the class
\xi\cdots\xi e\in H_{*}(\text{Conf}(\mathbb{R}^{2});\mathbb{F}_{2})
. Since
\left[x,x\right]=0
for all
x\in H_{*}(\text{Conf}(\mathbb{R}^{2});\mathbb{F}_{2})
(Property
3
of the Browder bracket), we have that
\left[e,e\right]=0
. Since

\left[e,x_{1}\right]=\left[e,\xi e\right]=\left[e,\left[e,e\right]\right]

(by Property
8
of the Browder bracket) and
\left[e,e\right]=0
, we have that
\left[x_{1},e\right]=0
. Since
x_{i}=\xi x_{i-1}
, the same argument recursively shows that
\left[x_{i},e\right]=0
.

We now deal with the case that
p
is odd. we first show that
\left[z_{i},e\right]=0
for all
i
. Recall that
z_{i}=\xi\cdots\xi\left[e,e\right]
and
y_{i}=\beta\xi\cdots\xi\left[e,e\right]
.
By the Jacobi identity of the Browder bracket (Property
3
of the Browder bracket),
\left[e,\left[e,e\right]\right]=\left[e,z_{0}\right]=0
.
By the relation of the top homology operation
\xi
and the Browder bracket and the Jacobi identity (Properties
8
and
3
of the Browder bracket),
\left[e,z_{i}\right]
can be recursively expressed as

\displaystyle\left[e,z_{i}\right]

\displaystyle=\left[e,\xi z_{i-1}\right]

\displaystyle=\text{ad}^{p}(z_{i-1})(e)\text{ by Property }\ref{iterated Browder bracket}\text{ of the Browder bracket}

\displaystyle=\left[z_{i-1},\left[z_{i-1},\cdots,\left[z_{i-1},\left[z_{i-1},e\right]\right]\cdots\right]\right]\text{ by definition of }\text{ad}^{p}.

Since
\left[z_{0},e\right]=0
, we have that
\left[z_{i-1},e\right]=0
by recursion
and so
\left[z_{i},e\right]=0
.

We now show that
\left[y_{i},e\right]=0
for
i>0
. First, we show that
\left[y_{1},e\right]=0
. Recall the homology operation
\zeta(-)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\beta\xi(-)-\text{ad}^{p-1}(-)(\beta(-))
and that
\left[x,\zeta y\right]=0
by Property
8
of the Browder bracket. Since
y_{i}=\beta\xi z_{i-1}
and the Browder bracket is linear in both entries, we have that

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\left[e,\zeta z_{i-1}\right]=\left[e,\beta\xi(z_{i-1})-\text{ad}^{p-1}(z_{i-1})(\beta(z_{i-1}))\right]

\displaystyle=\left[e,y_{i}-\text{ad}^{p-1}(z_{i-1})(\beta(z_{i-1}))\right]

\displaystyle=\left[e,y_{i}\right]-\left[e,\text{ad}^{p-1}(z_{i-1})(\beta(z_{i-1}))\right].

Therefore, it suffices to show that
\left[e,\text{ad}^{p-1}(z_{i-1})(\beta(z_{i-1}))\right]=0
. For
i=1
, we have that
\beta(z_{i-1})=\beta(z_{0})=\beta(\left[e,e\right])
. By Property
7
of the Browder bracket,

\beta\left[e,e\right]=\left[\beta e,e\right]\pm\left[e,\beta e\right].

We have that
\beta e=0
since
e
is a homology class of degree
0
. Therefore,
\beta z_{0}=0
and so
\left[e,y_{1}\right]=0
.

Suppose now that
i>1
. We have that
\beta z_{i-1}=y_{i-1}
since
i>1
. Note that

\displaystyle\left[e,\text{ad}^{p-1}(z_{i-1})(y_{i-1})\right]

\displaystyle=\left[e,\text{ad}(z_{i-1})(\text{ad}^{p-2}(z_{i-1})(y_{i-1}))\right]

\displaystyle=\left[e,\left[z_{i-1},\text{ad}^{p-2}(z_{i-1})(y_{i-1})\right]\right]

By the Jacobi identity (Property
3
of the Browder bracket), we have that

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\pm\left[e,\left[z_{i-1},\text{ad}^{p-2}(z_{i-1})(y_{i-1})\right]\right]\pm\left[z_{i-1},\left[e,\text{ad}^{p-2}(z_{i-1})(y_{i-1})\right]\right]

\displaystyle\pm\left[\text{ad}^{p-2}(z_{i-1})(y_{i-1}),\left[z_{i-1},e\right]\right].

Since
\left[z_{i-1},e\right]=0
, it suffices to show that
\left[z_{i-1},\left[e,\text{ad}^{p-2}(z_{i-1})(y_{i-1})\right]\right]=0
. By iterating the Jacobi identity, it suffices to show that

\left[e,\text{ad}(z_{i-1})(y_{i-1})\right]=\left[e,\left[z_{i-1},y_{i-1}\right]\right]=0.

But this follows from an another application of the Jacobi identity

0=\pm\left[e,\left[z_{i-1},y_{i-1}\right]\right]\pm\left[z_{i-1},\left[e,y_{i-1}\right]\right]\pm\left[y_{i-1},\left[z_{i-1},e\right]\right]

and the fact that
\left[e,z_{i-1}\right]
and
\left[e,y_{i-1}\right]
are both zero (
\left[e,y_{i-1}\right]
is zero by recursion).
∎

Proposition 6.12
.

Suppose that
M
is a closed connected surface. Let
p
be an odd prime and let
D(p,m,k)
denote the constant from
Proposition

2.11
. The stabilization map

t_{e^{p},c}\colon\text{Tot}(C\mathcal{X}^{1}_{k,\bullet}({T(V)_{*}},{S^{n-1}},{M\setminus D^{n}};{\mathbb{F}_{p}}))\to\text{Tot}(C\mathcal{X}^{1}_{k+p,\bullet}({\text{Mod-Conf}^{\,1}_{S^{n-1}}(\bar{D}^{n})},{S^{n-1}},{M\setminus D^{n}};{\mathbb{F}_{p}}))

from
Definition

5.33
induces a map

t_{e^{p},c}\colon H_{i}(\text{Conf}_{k}(M);\mathbb{F}_{p})\to H_{i}(\text{Conf}_{k+p}(M);\mathbb{F}_{p})

that is an isomorphism for
i<D(p,1,k)
and a surjection for
i=D(p,1,k)
.

Proof.

Fix a closed disk
\bar{D}^{n}
in
M
.
Showing that

t_{e^{p},c}\colon H_{i}(\text{Conf}_{k}(M);\mathbb{F}_{p})\to H_{i}(\text{Conf}_{k+p}(M);\mathbb{F}_{p})
is an isomorphism for
i<D(p,1,k)
and a surjection for
i=D(p,1,k)

is equivalent to showing that

H_{i}(\text{Cone}_{k}^{1,[1]}(M,(t_{e^{p},c}));\mathbb{F}_{p})
is zero for
i\leq D(p,1,k)
.

By
Lemma

6.9
, the stabilization map
t_{e^{p},c}
extends to a map of semi-simplicial chain complexes

(t_{e^{p},c})_{\bullet}\colon CZ^{1}_{k,\bullet}(T(V)_{*},M;\mathbb{F}_{p})\to CZ^{1}_{k+p,\bullet}(C_{*}(\text{Mod-Conf}^{\,1}_{S^{n-1}}(\bar{D}^{n});\mathbb{F}_{p}),M;\mathbb{F}_{p}).

We
can apply a relative version of the geometric realization spectral sequence:

\displaystyle E^{1}_{s,t}

\displaystyle=H_{t}\big(CZ^{1}_{k+p,s}(C_{*}(\text{Mod-Conf}^{\,1}_{S^{n-1}}(\bar{D}^{n});\mathbb{F}_{p}),M;\mathbb{F}_{p}),CZ^{1}_{k,s}(T(V)_{*},M;\mathbb{F}_{p})\big)

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow H_{s+t}(\text{Cone}_{k+p}^{1,[1]}(M,(\hat{t}_{e^{p},c}));\mathbb{F}_{p}).

We have that
\text{Tot}(CZ^{1}_{k,\bullet}(A^{I}_{*},M;\mathbb{F}_{p}))
is quasi-isomorphic to
\text{Tot}(C\mathcal{X}^{1}_{k,\bullet}({A^{I}_{*}},{S^{n-1}},{M\setminus D^{n}};{\mathbb{F}_{p}}))
. Therefore, to show that
H_{i}(\text{Cone}_{k+p}^{1,[1]}(M,(\hat{t}_{e^{p},c})))
, which is isomorphic to

H_{i}\big(\text{Tot}(C\mathcal{X}^{1}_{k+p,\bullet}({\bar{D}^{n}},{S^{n-1}},{M\setminus D^{n}};{\mathbb{F}_{p}})),\text{Tot}(C\mathcal{X}^{1}_{k,\bullet}({T(V)_{*}},{S^{n-1}},{M\setminus D^{n}};{\mathbb{F}_{p}}))\big),

vanishes for
i\leq D(p,1,k)
, it suffices to show that the relative homology

H_{t}\big(CZ^{1}_{k+p,s}(C_{*}(\text{Mod-Conf}^{\,1}_{S^{n-1}}(\bar{D}^{n});\mathbb{F}_{p}),M;\mathbb{F}_{p}),CZ^{1}_{k,s}(T(V)_{*},M;\mathbb{F}_{p})\big)

vanishes for
s+t=i\leq D(p,1,k)
. Since the manifolds
M\setminus\bar{D}^{n}\setminus\{m_{0},\ldots,m_{s}\}
appearing in

\text{Tot}(C\mathcal{X}^{1}_{k,\bullet}({A^{I}_{*}},{S^{n-1}},{M\setminus D^{n}\setminus\{m_{0},\ldots m_{s}\}};{\mathbb{F}_{p}}))\subset CZ^{1}_{k,s}(A^{I}_{*},M;\mathbb{F}_{p})

are open for all nonnegative
s
, by
Proposition

5.36
,
Corollary

5.38
, and
Proposition

3.6
,
E^{1}_{s,t}
vanishes for
s\leq D(p,1,k)
and hence also for
s+t\leq D(p,1,k)
.
∎

By applying the argument of
Proposition

6.12
, we can obtain additional stability results for the configuration spaces of a closed manifold and prove
Theorem

1.2
in the case that homology is with
\mathbb{F}_{p}
coefficients.

Proposition 6.13
.

Suppose that
M
is a connected manifold of dimension
n\geq 2
.
Let
D(p,m,k)
denote the constant from
Proposition

2.11
. Let
e\in H_{0}(\text{Conf}_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))
be the class of a point.

(1)

The map

t_{e,c}\colon H_{i}(\text{Conf}_{k}(M);\mathbb{F}_{2})\to H_{i}(\text{Conf}_{k+1}(M);\mathbb{F}_{2})

is an isomorphism for
i<k/2
and a surjection for
i=k/2
.

(2)

Suppose that
n=2
.
Let
x_{j}\in H_{*}(\text{Conf}(\mathbb{R}^{2});\mathbb{F}_{2})
denote the classes from
Theorem

2.8
. Let
\text{Cone}_{k}^{m,I}(M,(t_{\displaystyle x_{j},c}))
denote the m-th iterated mapping cone associated to the
m
-tuple

(t_{\displaystyle e^{p},c},t_{\displaystyle x_{1},c},\ldots,t_{\displaystyle x_{m-1},c})
and the subset
I\subseteq[m+1]
, with
I=[m]
or
[m+1]
.
The stabilization map

t_{\displaystyle x_{m},c}\colon\text{Tot}(C\mathcal{X}^{m+1}_{k,\bullet}({A^{[m+1]}_{*}},{S^{n-1}},{M\setminus D^{n}};{\mathbb{F}_{p}}))\to\text{Tot}(C\mathcal{X}^{m+1}_{k+2^{m},\bullet}({A^{[m]}_{*}},{S^{n-1}},{M\setminus D^{n}};{\mathbb{F}_{p}}))

induces a map

t_{\displaystyle x_{m},c}\colon H_{i}\big(\text{Cone}_{k}^{m,[m+1]}(M,(t_{\displaystyle x_{j},c}));\mathbb{F}_{2}\big)\to H_{i+2^{m}-1}\big(\text{Cone}_{k+2^{m}}^{m,[m]}(M,(t_{\displaystyle x_{j},c}));\mathbb{F}_{2}\big)

that is an isomorphism for
i<D(2,m+1,k)
and a surjection for
i=D(2,m+1,k)
.

(3)

Suppose that
n
is
2
and that
p
is an odd prime.
Let
y_{j}\in H_{*}(\text{Conf}(\mathbb{R}^{2});\mathbb{F}_{p})
denote the classes from
Theorem

2.8
.
Let
\text{Cone}_{k}^{m,I}(M,(t_{\displaystyle y_{j},c}))
denote the m-th iterated mapping cone associated to the
m
-tuple
(t_{\displaystyle e^{p},c},t_{\displaystyle y_{1},c},\ldots,t_{\displaystyle y_{m-1},c})
and the subset
I\subseteq[m+1]
, with
I=[m]
or
[m+1]
.

The map

t_{\displaystyle y_{m},c}\colon H_{i}\big(\text{Cone}_{k}^{m,[m+1]}(M,(t_{\displaystyle y_{j},c}));\mathbb{F}_{p}\big)\to H_{i+2p^{m}-2}\big(\text{Cone}_{k+2p^{m}}^{m,[m]}(M,(t_{\displaystyle y_{j},c}));\mathbb{F}_{p}\big)

is an isomorphism for
i<D(p,m+1,k)
and a surjection for
i=D(p,m+1,k)
.

(4)

Suppose that
n
is odd. The map

t_{e,c}\colon H_{i}(\text{Conf}_{k}(M);\mathbb{Z})\to H_{i}(\text{Conf}_{k+1}(M);\mathbb{Z})

is an isomorphism for
i<k/2
and a surjection for
i=k/2
.

(5)

Suppose that
n
is odd. Suppose that
R
is
\mathbb{Q}
or
\mathbb{F}_{p}
with
p>2
. The map

t_{e,c}\colon H_{i}(\text{Conf}_{k}(M);R)\to H_{i}(\text{Conf}_{k+1}(M);R)

is an isomorphism for
i<k
and a surjection for
i=k
.

(6)

Suppose that
n
is greater than
2
.
Let
\omega_{j}\in H_{*}(\text{Conf}(\mathbb{R}^{n});\mathbb{F}_{2})
denote the classes from
Proposition

3.16
.
Let
\text{Cone}_{k}^{m,I}(M,(t_{\displaystyle\omega_{j},c}))
denote the m-th iterated mapping cone associated to the
m
-tuple

(t_{\displaystyle e,c},t_{\displaystyle\omega_{1},c},\ldots,t_{\displaystyle\omega_{m-1},c})
and the subset
I\subseteq[m+1]
, with
I=[m]
or
[m+1]
.

The map

t_{\displaystyle\omega_{m},c}\colon H_{i}\big(\text{Cone}_{k}^{m,[m+1]}(M,(t_{\displaystyle\omega_{j},c}));\mathbb{F}_{2}\big)\to H_{i+2^{m}-1}\big(\text{Cone}_{k+2^{m}}^{m,[m]}(M,(t_{\displaystyle\omega_{j},c}));\mathbb{F}_{2}\big)

is an isomorphism for
i<D(2,m+1,k)
and a surjection for
i=D(2,m+1,k)
.

(7)

Suppose that
n
is even and greater than
2
and that
p
is an odd prime.
The map

t_{e^{p},c}\colon H_{i}(\text{Conf}_{k}(M);\mathbb{F}_{p})\to H_{i}(\text{Conf}_{k+p}(M);\mathbb{F}_{p})

is an isomorphism for
i<k
and a surjection for
i=k
.

Proof.

The argument is exactly the same as
the proof of
Proposition

6.12
.
The only difference is that we implicitly use the fact that for an open connected manifold
M
, there is a zig-zag of quasi-isomorphisms
H_{*}(\text{Cone}^{m+1,[m+1]}(M,(t_{z_{i},c}));R)\to H_{*}(\text{Cone}^{m+1}(M,(t_{z_{i}}));R)

by a stronger version of
Proposition

5.36
for iterated mapping cones (the proof of this stronger version is similar to the proof of
Proposition

5.36
, so we omit it).
The range for Part
1
follows from
Proposition

3.6
and
Proposition

3.17
.
The ranges for Parts
2
and
3
follow from
Corollary

3.13
.
The ranges for Parts
4
,
5
, and
7
follow from
Theorem

3.18
. The ranges for Part
6
follow from
Proposition

3.17
.
∎

When the dimension of the manifold is odd, one can apply Parts
5
and
6
to obtain the following secondary homological stability result for the integral relative homology
H_{*}(\text{Conf}_{k}(M),\text{Conf}_{k-1}(M);\mathbb{Z})
, which proves
Theorem

1.2
in the case that homology is integral homology, exactly like
Proposition

3.19
.

Corollary 6.14
.

Suppose that
M
is a connected manifold of odd dimension greater than
2
.
The map

t_{\displaystyle\omega_{1},c}\colon C_{i}(\text{Conf}_{k}(M),\text{Conf}_{k-1}(M);\mathbb{Z})\to C_{i+1}(\text{Conf}_{k+2}(M),\text{Conf}_{k+1}(M);\mathbb{Z})

induces an isomorphism for
i<D(2,2,k)
and a surjection for
i=D(2,2,k)
.

We now prove
Theorem

1.2
.

Proof of
Theorem

1.2
.

Theorem

1.1
immediately follows from
Proposition

6.13
and
Corollary

6.14
in the case
m=1
.
∎

Remark 6.15
.

If
M
is a closed connected manifold of even dimension, we do not know if there is a similar statement to
Corollary

6.14
. In this case, the issue is that we do not know how to define relative homology
H_{*}(\text{Conf}_{k}(M),\text{Conf}_{k-1}(M);\mathbb{Z})
.
When
M
is a closed connected manifold of even dimension, we can only consider
H_{*}(\text{Conf}_{k}(M),\text{Conf}_{k-p}(M);\mathbb{F}_{p})
, not
H_{*}(\text{Conf}_{k}(M),\text{Conf}_{k-1}(M);\mathbb{F}_{p})
, and there is no analogue of
H_{*}(\text{Conf}_{k}(M),\text{Conf}_{k-p}(M);\mathbb{F}_{p})
for integral homology.

It might still be possible to obtain a secondary stability statement result by working in the setting of divided power algebras. Let
D
denote the divided power algebra over
\mathbb{Z}
in a single variable. Suppose that
M
is an open manifold. The chain complex
C_{*}(\text{Conf}(M);\mathbb{Z})
can be viewed as a
\mathbb{Z}[x]
-module, where the variable
x
acts on
C_{*}(\text{Conf}(M);\mathbb{Z})
by stabilization,
and we have that

\text{Tor}_{i}^{\mathbb{Z}[x]}(C_{*}(\text{Conf}(M);\mathbb{Z}),\mathbb{Z})\cong\bigoplus_{k}H_{i}(\text{Conf}_{k}(M),\text{Conf}_{k-1}(M);\mathbb{Z}).

When
M
is a closed manifold,
\mathbb{Z}[x]
no longer acts on
C_{*}(\text{Conf}(M);\mathbb{Z})
, but
D
acts on
C^{*}(\text{Conf}(M);\mathbb{Z})
. So instead consider
the hyper Tor groups
\text{Tor}_{i}^{D}(C^{*}(\text{Conf}(M);\mathbb{Z}),\mathbb{Z})
. These hyper Tor groups are an analogue of the relative homology
H_{i}(\text{Conf}_{k}(M),\text{Conf}_{k-1}(M);\mathbb{Z})
. We conjecture that the hyper Tor groups
\text{Tor}_{*}^{D}(C^{*}(\text{Conf}(M);\mathbb{Z}),\mathbb{Z})
stabilize for a closed manifold
M
. We also ask if there is an analogue of the secondary stabilization map

t_{2}\colon C_{*}(\text{Conf}_{k}(M),\text{Conf}_{k-1}(M);\mathbb{Z})\to C_{*+1}(\text{Conf}_{k+2}(M),\text{Conf}_{k+1}(M);\mathbb{Z})

for the hyper Tor groups
\text{Tor}_{*}^{D}(C^{*}(\text{Conf}(M);\mathbb{Z}),\mathbb{Z})
.

Remark 6.16
.

In the case that
M
is a manifold of finite type and homology is with coefficients over the field
\mathbb{F}_{2}
, the secondary stability results for this case can also be deduced from
[
BCT89
]
. Bödigheimer–Cohen–Taylor show that the homology
H_{*}(\text{Conf}(M);\mathbb{F}_{2})
can be explicitly calculated from a graded vector space
\mathscr{C}(H_{*}(M;\mathbb{F}_{2}))
depending only on the homology
H_{*}(M;\mathbb{F}_{2})
and the dimension of
M
(see
[
BCT89
, Theorem A]
). They give an explicit filtration of
\mathscr{C}(H_{*}(M;\mathbb{F}_{2}))
which calculates
H_{*}(\text{Conf}_{k}(M);\mathbb{F}_{2})
(see
[
BCT89
, Theorem B and Section 4]
).

As a result, we can use the vector space
\mathscr{C}(H_{*}(M;\mathbb{F}_{2}))
to deduce secondary stability in this case. Since the stabilization map
t_{1}\colon H_{*}(\text{Conf}_{k}(M);\mathbb{F}_{2})\to H_{*}(\text{Conf}_{k+1}(M);\mathbb{F}_{2})
is injective when
M
is an open manifold, the relative homology
H_{*}(\text{Conf}_{k}(M),\text{Conf}_{k-1}(M);\mathbb{F}_{2})
is the cokernel of this stabilization map. Therefore, secondary stability can almost certainly be deduced from the vector space
\mathscr{C}(H_{*}(M;\mathbb{F}_{2}))
in this case. The only hard part is checking that the obvious guess for the secondary stabilization map on
\mathscr{C}(H_{*}(M;\mathbb{F}_{2}))
agrees with the secondary stabilization map
t_{2}\colon H_{*}(\text{Conf}_{k}(M),\text{Conf}_{k-1}(M);\mathbb{F}_{2})\to H_{*+1}(\text{Conf}_{k+2}(M),\text{Conf}_{k+1}(M);\mathbb{F}_{2})
.
